As $x=\lim y_{x,m}^i$ and $y_x^i=\lim  \tilde{y}_{x,m}^i$, by the semicontinuity of length \cite{golab}
                                                                                                                                     
\begin{align*}
\operatorname{dist}(z,T) &= \lim_{m\to\infty}\operatorname{dist}(z_m,T)\\ 
&\geq C_{T}^{-1}\liminf_{m\to\infty}\min\{\ell(\sigma_{x,m}^i[z_m,y_{x,m}^i]),\ell(\sigma_{x,m}^i[z_m,\tilde{y}_{x,m}^i])\} \\
&\geq C_{T}^{-1}\min\{\ell(\gamma_{x}^i[x,z]),\ell(\gamma_{x}^i[y_x^i,z])\}.
\end{align*}

For each $x\in {\bf W}^*$, each $k\geq \operatorname{\mathtt{L}}(x)$, and each $i\in\{1,2\}$, define $\gamma_{x,k}^i$ to be the closure of the connected component of $\gamma_{x}^i \cap \Omega_k$ that contains $x$. Let also $q_{x,k}^i$ be the endpoint of $\gamma_{x,k}^i$ on $\Gamma_k$. Claim (ii) is immediate. 

To show (i), fix $x\in {\bf W}^*$, $k\geq \operatorname{\mathtt{L}}(x)$, $i\in \{1,2\}$, and $z\in \gamma_x^i(x,q_{x,k}^i)$. For each $m\in\mathbb{N}$, there exists $w_m \in \sigma_{x,m}^i$ and $z_m \in \sigma_{x,m}^i[y_{x,m}^i,w_m]$, such that $z_m \to z$ and $w_m \to q_{x,k}^i$ as $m\to \infty$. By the $C_{T}$-John property and semicontinuity of length,  
\begin{align*}
0 < \min\{|z - x|, |z - q_{x,k}^i|\} &\leq \min\{\ell(\gamma_x^i[z, x]), \ell(\gamma_x^i[z, q_{x,k}^{i}])\} \\
&\leq \liminf_{m \to \infty} \min\{\ell([y_{m,i}, z_m]), \ell([w_{m,i}, z_m])\} \\
&\leq C_{T} \lim_{m \to \infty} \operatorname{dist}(z_m, T)\\ 
&= C_{T} \operatorname{dist}(z, T)
\end{align*}
which shows that $z\not\in T$. This completes the proof of (i).

To show (iii), fix $x\in {\bf W}^*$, $k\geq \operatorname{\mathtt{L}}(x)$, and $i\in \{1,2\}$. By Lemma \ref{lem:properties of thickening}(ii) and the fact that $\operatorname{diam} T=1$ we get $\operatorname{diam}\Gamma_1\leq 2$ and hence $\operatorname{dist}(\partial B,\Gamma_1)>23$. By Lemma \ref{lem:def of thickening of Tm,eta}, $q_{x,k}^i\in\partial \mathscr{T}_{\delta_Z}(Z)$ for some $Z\in{\bf X}^k$. Moreover, $\ell(\gamma_{x}^i[x,q_{x,k}^i]) \leq \ell(\gamma_x^i[q_{x,k}^i,y_x^i])$ since otherwise Lemma \ref{lem:properties of thickening}(ii) and Lemma \ref{lem:def of thickening of Tm,eta} give
\begin{align*}
23<\operatorname{dist} (\partial B,\Gamma_1)\leq \operatorname{dist} (\partial B,\overline{\Omega_k})\leq |y_{x}^i-q_{x,k}^i|&\leq \ell(\gamma_x^i[q_{x,k}^i,y_x^i])\\ &\leq C_{T}\operatorname{dist}(q_{x,k}^i,T)\\ &\leq 8C_{T}\delta_Z
\end{align*}
which contradicts \eqref{eq:def of delta X}. Therefore, by the John property of $\gamma_x^i$ and Lemma \ref{lem:properties of thickening}(ii)
\begin{align*} 
\ell(\gamma_{x,k}^i) = \ell(\gamma_x^i[x,q_{x,k}^i]) = \min \{\ell(\gamma_x^i[x,q_{x,k}^i]), \ell(\gamma_x^i[y_{x}^i,q_{x,k}^i])\} &\leq C_T\operatorname{dist}(q_{x,k}^i,{\bf T})\\
&\leq 8C_T\delta_Z.
\end{align*}
Last, we claim that $Z\in\{X_{x,k}^1,X_{x,k}^2\}$. Assume, for a contradiction, that $Z\notin\{X_{x,k}^1,X_{x,k}^2\}$. By Lemma \ref{lem:properties of thickening}(ii) there exists $z\in Z$ such that $|q_{x,k}^i-z|\leq 8\delta_Z$. If $Z\cap (X_{x,k}^1 \cup X_{x,k}^2)=\emptyset$, then by Definition \ref{def:tile decomposition}(iii) we have
\begin{align*}
8C_T\delta_Z\geq \ell(\gamma_{x,k}^i)\geq |x-q_{x,k}^i|\geq |x-z|-|z-q_{x,k}^i|&\geq \operatorname{dist} (X_{x,k}^1,Z)-8\delta_Z\\ 
&\geq C^{-1}\operatorname{diam} Z-8\delta_Z
\end{align*}
which contradicts \eqref{eq:def of delta X}. If $Z\cap X_{x,k}^j\neq\emptyset $ for some $j\in \{1,2\}$, then Lemma \ref{lem:properties of BM}(ii) asserts the existence of $w\in T(x,z]\cap \partial X_{x,k}^j$. Definition \ref{def:tile decomposition}(ii), Lemma \ref{lem:properties of BM}(vii), and the bounded turning property of $T$ give
\begin{align*}
8C_T\delta_Z\ge\ell(\gamma_{x,k}^i)\geq |x-q_{x,k}^i|\geq |x-z|-|z-q_{x,k}^i|&\geq \frac{\operatorname{diam} T[x,z]}{C_T}-8\delta_Z \\ 
&\geq\frac{|x-w|}{C_T}-8\delta_Z \\ 
&\geq\frac{\operatorname{diam} X_{x,k}^j}{C_TC^2}-8\delta_Z\\ 
&\geq \frac{\operatorname{diam} Z}{C_TC^3}-8\delta_Z,
\end{align*}
which contradicts \eqref{eq:def of delta X}. The proof the claim is complete. Last, by \eqref{eq:def of delta X} and Definition \ref{def:tile decomposition}(ii) we have that $\delta_{X_{x,k}^{i}}< 2C\delta_{X_{x,k}^j}$ for $i,j\in \{1,2\}$ distinct. Thus, claim (iii) follows.

To show (iv), fix $x\in{\bf W}^*$ and $k\geq\operatorname{\mathtt{L}}(x)$. We claim that $B(x,r_{x,k})\cap \mathscr{T}_{\delta_Z}(Z)=\emptyset$ for all $Z\in{\bf X}^{k}\setminus\{X_{x,k}^1,X_{x,k}^2\}$. Assuming the claim, (iv) follows from Lemma \ref{lem:def of thickening of Tm,eta}. To show the claim, fix $Z\in{\bf X}^{k}\setminus\{X_{x,k}^1,X_{x,k}^2\}$ and $z\in \mathscr{T}_{\delta_Z}(Z)$. By Lemma \ref{lem:properties of thickening}(ii) there exists $z'\in Z$ such that $|z-z'|<8\delta_Z$. Since $Z\neq X_{x,k}^1, X_{x,k}^2$, Lemma \ref{lem:properties of BM}(ii) implies that $T[z',x)\cap\partial X_{x,k}^j=\{v\}$ for some $j\in \{1,2\}$. Fix $y\in \overline{B}(x,r_{x,k})$. If $Z\cap X_{x,k}^j\neq\emptyset$, then by the bounded turning property of $T$, \eqref{eq:def of delta X}, Definition \ref{def:tile decomposition}(ii), and Lemma \ref{lem:properties of BM}(vii) we have
\begin{align*}
|z-y|\geq |z'-x|-|z-z'|-|x-y|&\geq\frac{\operatorname{diam} T[z',x]}{C_T}-8\delta_Z-r_{x,k}\\ 
&\geq \frac{|x-v|}{C_T}-16C\delta_{X_{x,k}^j}-r_{x,k}\\ 
&\geq \frac{\operatorname{diam} X_{x,k}^j}{C_TC^2}-16C\delta_{X_{x,k}^j}-r_{x,k}>0.
\end{align*}
Suppose now that $Z\cap X_{x,k}^j=\emptyset$. Without loss of generality, we may assume that $\delta_{X_{x,k}^j}\geq \delta_Z$ Then by \eqref{eq:def of delta X} and Definition \ref{def:tile decomposition}(iii) we have
\begin{align*}
|z-y|\geq |z'-x|-|z-z'|-|x-y| &\geq \operatorname{dist} (Z,X_{x,k}^j)-8\delta_Z-r_{x,k}\\ 
&\geq C^{-1}\operatorname{diam} X_{x,k}^j-16C\delta_{X_{x,k}^j}-r_{x,k}>0.
\end{align*}

To show (v), fix $x\in {\bf W}^*$, $k\geq \operatorname{\mathtt{L}}(x)$ and $i\in \{1,2\}$. For each $r\leq r_{x,k}$, Lemma \ref{lem:properties of BM}(vi) and \eqref{eq:def of delta X} give points $p_{r},q_{r}\in\partial B(x,r)\cap T$ such that $x\in T(p_r,q_r)\subset B(x,r)$. Denote by $U_{x,r}^1,U_{x,r}^2$ the connected components of $B(x,r)\setminus T[p_r,q_r]$. By (i), $\gamma_{x,k}^i\cap T=\{x\}$. Thus, if $\xi_{x,r}^i$ denotes the connected component of $B(x,r)\cap \gamma_{x,k}^i$ that contains $x$, then we may assume that $\xi_{x,r}^i\subset U_{x,r}^i\cup\{x\}$. The first part of claim (v) is proven. By (iii), \eqref{eq:def of delta X}, and the definition of $r_{x,k}$ now we have that $\gamma_{x,k}^i\subset B(x,r_{x,k})$. Therefore, by (i) we may assume again that $\gamma_{x,k}^i\subset U_{x,r_k}^i\cup\{x\}$. The second claim of (v) is proven. Finally, since $U_{x,r_k}^1\cap U_{x,r_k}^2=\emptyset$ we have $\gamma_{x,k}^1\cap\gamma_{x,k}^2=\{x\}$.

To show (vi), fix $x\in {\bf W}^*$ and $k\geq \operatorname{\mathtt{L}}(x)$. By (i) and (v), $\gamma_{x,k}^1\cup \gamma_{x,k}^2$ is an arc in $\overline{\Omega_k}$ that intersects $T$ at exactly $x$, has both endpoints on $\Gamma_k$ and the rest of the arc (minus the endpoints and $x$) is in $\Omega_k \setminus T$. Therefore, $\Omega_k\setminus (\gamma_{x,k}^1\cup \gamma_{x,k}^2)$ contains exactly two components $O_1,O_2$ which are Jordan domains. Clearly $B_x^1\cup B_x^2 \subset O_1\cup O_2$. Assume for a contradiction that $B_x^1\cup B_x^2\subset O_1$. Let $r=\frac12\min_{Z\in{\bf X}^k}\delta_Z$ and $q_1,q_2\in \partial B(x,r)\cap T$ such that $x\in T(q_1,q_2)\subset B(x,r)$. By Lemma \ref{lem:properties of thickening}(ii) and Lemma \ref{lem:def of thickening of Tm,eta}, $B(x,r)\subset \Omega_k$. Thus, $q_{x,k}^1,q_{x,k}^2\notin B(x,r)$. For each $i\in\{1,2\}$, let $t_i\in\partial B(x,r)\cap\gamma_{x,k}^i$ be the first point of $\gamma_{x,k}^i$ that meets $\partial B(x,r)$ as we travel from $x$ to $q_{x,k}^i$. By (v), the arcs $\gamma_{x,k}^1(x,t_1),\gamma_{x,k}^2(x,t_2)$ are in different connected components of $B(x,r)\setminus T[q_1,q_2]$. Denote by $V_1,V_2$ the connected components of $B(x,r)\setminus (\gamma_{x,k}^1[x,t_1)\cup\gamma_{x,k}^2[x,t_2))$ and relabeling we may assume that $q_i \in \partial V_i$ and $V_i\subset O_i$. Fix $u_1\in \gamma_{x,k}^1(x,t_1)$ and $u_2\in \gamma_{x,k}^2(x,t_2)$. By Lemma \ref{lem:Jordan domain prerequisite} there exists arc $\sigma$ with endpoints $u_1,u_2$ and other than its endpoints, it is contained in $V_2$. But then, $T[q_2,x)\cap (\sigma_1\setminus\{u_1,u_2\})\neq\emptyset$ which contradicts $O_2\cap T=\emptyset$. Thus, relabeling, we may assume that $B_x^1\subset O_1$ and $B_x^2\subset O_2$, and (i) gives that $O_i\cap T=B_x^i$ for $i=1,2$.

To show (vii), fix $x,y\in {\bf W}^*$ distinct, $k\geq \operatorname{\mathtt{L}}(x)$, $l\geq\operatorname{\mathtt{L}}(y)$, and $i,j\in \{1,2\}$. Without loss of generality, we may assume that $l\geq k$. By Lemma \ref{lem:properties of BM}, $x,y\in{\bf V}^{l+1}$. Since $x\neq y$, Definition \ref{def:tile decomposition}(i) and Lemma \ref{lem:properties of BM}(ii) assert that there exist $i_1,i_2\in\{1,2\}$ such that $X_{x,l+1}^{i_1}\cap X_{y,l+1}^{i_2}=\emptyset$. Without loss of generality, suppose that $X_{x,l+1}^1\cap X_{y,l+1}^1=\emptyset$ and that for some $a\in \{1,2\}$, 
$$\delta_{X_{x,l+1}^a}=\max\{\delta_ {X_{x,l+1}^1},\delta_{X_{x,l+1}^2},\delta_ {X_{y,l+1}^1},\delta_{X_{y,l+1}^2}\}.$$
Fix also $z_1\in \gamma_{x,k}^i[x,q_{x,k}^i]$ and $z_2\in \gamma_{y,l}^j[y,q_{y,l}^j]$.  By (iii), \eqref{eq:def of delta X}, and Definition \ref{def:tile decomposition}(ii),(iii) we have
\begin{align*}
|z_1-z_2|\geq |x-y|-|x-z_1|-|y-z_2|&\geq \frac{\operatorname{diam} X_{x,l+1}^{1}}{C}-32C_TC\delta_{X_{x,l+1}^a}\\
&\geq \frac{\operatorname{diam} X_{x,l+1}^a}{C^2}-32C_TC\delta_{X_{x,l+1}^a}\\ 
&>2C\delta_{X_{x,l+1}^a}.
\end{align*}
                                                                                                                                                                                  

To show (viii), let $i'\in\{1,2\}\setminus\{i\}$ and let $p,q\in \partial B(x,r_{x,k+1})\cap T$ such that $x\in T(p,q)\subset B(x,r_{x,k+1})$. Denote by $U_{x}^1,U_{x}^2$ the connected components of $B(x,r_{x,k+1})\setminus T[p,q]$. By (iii),(v) we may assume that $\gamma_{x,k}^i\subset U_{x}^i\cup\{x\}$ and that $\gamma_{x,k}^{i'}\subset U_{x}^{i'}\cup\{x\}$. Let $z_1\in\gamma_{x,k}^i$ and $z_2\in\omega_{x,k}^{i'}$ where $\omega_{x,k}^{i'}$ is the subarc of $\gamma_{x,k}^{i'}$ in $\overline{\Omega_{k}}\setminus\Omega_{k+1}$ with one endpoint in $\Gamma_{k}$. Since $B(x,r_{x,k+1})$ is a convex set, the line segment $[z_1,z_2]$ must intersect $T[p,q]$ at some point $u$. In conjunction with the fact that $z_2\notin \Omega_{k+1}$ it follows that there exists $p'\in [z_2,u]\cap \Gamma_{k+1}\cap B(x,r_{x,k+1})$. By (iv), Lemma \ref{lem:def of thickening of Tm,eta}, and the choice of $r_{x,k+1}$ we have that $p'\in \partial \mathscr{T}_{\delta_{X_{x,k+1}^{j_1}}}(X_{x,k+1}^{j_1})$ and that $u\in X_{x,k+1}^{j_2}$ for $j_1,j_2\in\{1,2\}$. By Lemma \ref{lem:def of thickening of Tm,eta}, $p'\notin \mathrm{int}(\mathscr{T}_{\delta_{X_{x,k+1}^{j_2}}}(X_{x,k+1}^{j_2}))$ and by Lemma \ref{lem:properties of thickening}(ii),
\[ |z_1-z_2|\geq |p'-u|> \delta_{X_{x,k+1}^{j_2}}\geq (2C)^{-1}\max\{\delta_{X_{x,k+1}^1},\delta_{X_{x,k+1}^2}\}.\qedhere \]
<!-- end proof-20 -->
<!-- rem-3: body_md -->
\label{rem: points in gamma n disjoint}
By Lemma \ref{lem:def of thickening of Tm,eta} for $n\neq m$ we have that $\Gamma_n\cap\Gamma_m=\emptyset$ which implies that $D_n\cap D_m=\emptyset$.
<!-- end rem-3 -->
<!-- lem-22: body_md -->
\label{lem:diameter of the sides of W.S1}
Let $n\in \mathbb{N}$, let $x,y\in D_n$ be adjacent, and let $v_x,v_y$ denote the associated $n$-vertices. 
\begin{enumerate}
\item If $v_x=v_y$, then there exists a unique leaf tile $X\in {\bf X}^n$ such that $v_{x}\in\partial X$.
\item If $v_x\neq v_y$, then there exists a unique tile $X\in {\bf X}^n$ such that $v_{x},v_y\in\partial X$.   
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lem-22 -->
<!-- proof-21: body_md -->
By Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(i),(v),(vi) the set
$$T[v_x,v_y]\cup \gamma_{v_y,n}^i\cup\Gamma_n[x,y]\cup \gamma_{v_x,n}^j$$
is a Jordan curve for $i,j\in\{1,2\}$. Let $O$ be the Jordan domain enclosed by it. By Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(i), $O\cap \Gamma_n=\emptyset$ and $\partial O \cap T \neq \emptyset$. Therefore, by Lemma \ref{lem:def of thickening of Tm,eta}, $O\subset \Omega_n$ and by Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(vi), $O\cap T$ is one of the two components of $T\setminus\{v\}$. 

Suppose first that $v_x=v_y=v$. By Lemma \ref{lem:properties of BM}(ii),(iii), $v$ belongs to exactly two distinct $n$-tiles; one being in $O\cap T$ and the other being in $T\setminus O$. Denote by $X$ the one of these two tiles that is in $O\cap T$. We claim that $X$ is a leaf tile. Suppose, otherwise, that there exists a point $w\in \partial X\setminus\{v\}$. Since $O\subset \Omega_n$, $\gamma_{w,n}^t\cap \partial O\neq\emptyset$ for some $t\in\{1,2\}$. By Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(i),(v) and the fact that $x,y$ are adjacent we reach a contradiction. Hence, $X$ is a leaf tile.

Suppose now that $v_x\neq v_y$. Let $X,Y$ be the unique $n$-tiles that contain $v_x$. Assume for a contradiction that $v_y\in  T\setminus(X\cup Y)$. By Lemma \ref{lem:properties of BM}(iii) there exists $u\in T(v_x,v_y]\cap (\partial X\cup\partial Y)$. Without loss of generality, we may assume that $u\in\partial X$. Let $r=\frac12\min_{Z\in{\bf X}^n}\delta_Z$ and let $p,q\in T[v_x,v_y]\cap \partial B(u,r)$ such that $u\in T(p,q)\subset B(u,r)$. Lemma \ref{lem:properties of thickening}(ii) and Lemma \ref{lem:def of thickening of Tm,eta} give $\overline{B}(u,r)\cap \Gamma_{n}=\emptyset$. Moreover, if $z_1\in\overline{B}(u,r)$ and $z_2\in \gamma_{v_x,n}^i$, then by Lemma \ref{lem:properties of BM}(vii), Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(iii), and \eqref{eq:def of delta X} we have 
$$|z_1-z_2|\geq |v_x-u|-|z_1-u|-|z_2-v_x|\geq \frac{\operatorname{diam} X}{C^2}-\frac{\delta_X}{2}-16CC_ T\delta_X>0.$$
If $z_2\in \gamma_{v_y,n}^j$, then let $Z$ be the $n$-tile such that $v_y\in \partial Z$ and $u'\in T[v_x,v_y)\cap \partial Z$. Lemma \ref{lem:properties of BM}(vii), Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(iii), \eqref{eq:def of delta X}, and the bounded turning property of $T$ give
\begin{align*}
|z_1-z_2|\geq |v_y-u|-|z_1-u|-|z_2-v_y|&\geq \frac{\operatorname{diam} T[u,v_y]}{C_T}-r-16CC_T\delta_Z\\ &\geq \frac{|u'-v_y|}{C_T}-\frac{\delta_Z}{2}-16CC_T\delta_Z\\ &\geq \frac{\operatorname{diam} Z}{C^2C_T}-17CC_T\delta_Z\\ &>0.
\end{align*}
Let now $p\in \partial B(u,r)\cap T[u,v_x]$ and $q\in \partial B(u,r)\cap T[u,v_y]$ such that $u\in T(p,q)\subset B(u,r)$. For each $l\in\{1,2\}$, let $\sigma_l$ be the component of $B(u,r)\cap \gamma_{x,n}^l$ that contains $x$. By Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(v), $\sigma_1,\sigma_2$ lie in different connected components of $B(u,r)\setminus T[p,q]$. Similar arguments with the proof of Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(vi) assert now that for some $j_0\in \{1,2\}$, $\sigma_{j_0}\setminus\{x\}\subset O$ and thus by Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(i), $\gamma_{u,n}^{j_0}\cap \partial O\neq\emptyset$. By Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(i),(viii) and the fact that $x,y$ are adjacent, we reach a contradiction.
<!-- end proof-21 -->
<!-- rem-4: body_md -->
If $x,y,v_x,v_y$ are as in Lemma \ref{lem:diameter of the sides of W.S1} and $v_x\neq v_y$, then the tile $X$ given by the lemma, can not be a leaf tile.
<!-- end rem-4 -->
<!-- lem-23: body_md -->
\label{lem:diameter of the sides of W.S}
Let $n\in \mathbb{N}$, let $x,y\in D_n$ be adjacent, let $v_x,v_y$ denote the associated $n$-vertices, and let $X$ be the $n$-tile given by Lemma \ref{lem:diameter of the sides of W.S1}.
\begin{enumerate}[(i)] 
\item If $O$ is the domain enclosed by the Jordan curve 
$$T[v_x,v_y]\cup \gamma_{v_y,n}^i\cup\Gamma_n[x,y]\cup \gamma_{v_x,n}^j,$$ 
then $O\subset \Omega_n$ and $O\cap T\subset X\setminus\partial X$. In particular, if $X$ is a leaf tile, then $O\cap T=X\setminus\partial X$.
\item For each $w\in\partial X$ and for each $n$-tile $Z$ with $Z\cap X\neq\emptyset$, 
$$ B(w,R_{X,n})\subset \mathscr{T}_{\delta_Z}(B(w,R_{X,n}))\subset B(w,2R_{X,n})$$
where
\[ R_{X,n}=2^{13}C^4C^2_{T}\min\{\delta_Z : Z\in {\bf X}^n, Z\cap X\neq\emptyset\}.\]
\item We have 
\[ \Gamma_n[x,y]\subset \mathscr{T}_{\delta_X}(X\setminus \bigcup_{w\in\partial X}B(w,R_{X,n}))\cup \bigcup_{w\in\partial X} B(w,2R_{X,n}).\] 
Moreover, if $W\in {\bf X}^n$ with $W\cap X\neq \emptyset$ and $\delta_W=\min_{Z\in {\bf X}^n, Z\cap X\neq\emptyset}\delta_Z$, then 
$$(2C)^{-1}\delta_W\leq \operatorname{diam} \Gamma_n[x,y]\leq 2C\operatorname{diam} W.$$ 
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lem-23 -->
<!-- proof-22: body_md -->
For (i), suppose that there exists $y\in O\cap (Z'\cup\partial X\setminus\{v_x,v_y\})$ for some $n$-tile $Z'\neq X$. Let $w\in \partial X$ be the first time that we meet $X$ as we travel from $y$ to $T[v_x,v_y]$. By Lemma \ref{lem:properties of BM}(vi) and Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(vi) it follows that $w\in O$. Similar arguments with Case 1 reach a contradiction. We conclude that $O\cap  T\subset X\setminus\partial X$. Note that by Definition \ref{def:tile decomposition}(i) $\inf_{Z\in{\bf X}^n, Z\cap X\neq\emptyset}\delta_X$ is minimized.  

Claim (ii) can be easily verified using Definition \ref{def:tile decomposition}(ii), Lemma \ref{lem:properties of thickening}(ii), and Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(iii).

To show (iii), let $t\in\Gamma_n[x,y]$ such that $t\in\mathscr{T}_{\delta_Z}(Z)$ for some $n$-tile $Z\neq X$. Suppose first that $Z\cap X=\emptyset$. Without loss of generality suppose that $\delta_Z\leq \delta_X$. By Lemma \ref{lem:properties of thickening}(ii) there exists $t'\in Z$ such that $|t-t'|<8\delta_Z$. Let $\alpha\subset \mathscr{T}_{\delta_Z}(Z)$ be a curve connecting $t$ to $t'$. By Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(vii) there exists an arc $\zeta\subset \Gamma_n$ connecting $q_{w,n}^1$ to $q_{w,n}^2$ for some $w\in\partial X$ so that $Z\setminus\{w\}$ lies inside the domain enclosed by the curve $\gamma_{w,n}^1\cup\zeta\cup\gamma_{w,n}^2$. Hence, the fact that $O\subset\Omega_n$, and (ii) show that $\alpha\cap B(w,R_{X,n})\neq\emptyset$. But if $z_1\in\mathscr{T}_{\delta_Z}(Z)$ and $z_2\in B(w,R_{X,n})$, then by Definition \ref{def:tile decomposition}(iii), the choice of $R_{X,n}$, and \eqref{eq:def of delta X} we have 
$$|z_1-z_2|\ge \operatorname{dist} (X,Z)-R_{X,n}-\delta_Z\geq C^{-1}\operatorname{diam} X-R_{X,n}-\delta_X>0$$
which is a contradiction.

Suppose now $Z\cap X\neq\emptyset$. We may assume that $t\in \mathscr{T}_{\delta_Z}(Z\setminus\bigcup_{w\in\partial X}B(w,R_{X,n}))$ since by (iii) and Lemma \ref{lem:properties of thickening}(iii),(iv) we have
$$\mathscr{T}_{\delta_Z}(Z)\subset \mathscr{T}_{\delta_Z}(Z\setminus\bigcup_{w\in\partial X}B(w,R_{X,n}))\cup \bigcup_{w\in\partial X}B(w,2R_{X,n}).$$
By Lemma \ref{lem:properties of thickening}(ii) there exists $t'\in Z\setminus\bigcup_{w\in\partial X}B(w,R_{X,n})$ such that $|t-t'|<8\delta_Z$. By Lemma \ref{lem:def of thickening of Tm,eta} there exists a $(2^6C_TC)$-bounded turning curve $\omega\subset\overline{\Omega_n}$ connecting $t$ to $t'$. Similar arguments with the case of $X\cap Y=\emptyset$ show that $\omega\cap B(w,R_{X,n})\neq\emptyset$. Since $t\in\omega\cap (\overline{\Omega_n}\setminus B(w,2R_{X,n}))$, Lemma \ref{lem:properties of thickening}(ii) and Definition \ref{def:tile decomposition}(ii) give
$$8C\delta_X\geq 4\delta_Z>\frac12|t-t'|\geq \frac{\operatorname{diam} \omega}{2^7C_TC}\geq \frac{\operatorname{dist}(B(w,2R_{X,n}),B(w,R_{X,n})}{2^7C_TC}\geq \frac{R_{X,n}}{2^7C_TC}$$
which is contradiction from the definition of $R_{X,n}$ and \eqref{eq:def of delta X}. Finally, by (ii) and Lemma \ref{lem:properties of thickening}(iii),(iv) we have
\begin{align*}
\Gamma_n[x,y]&\subset \mathscr{T}_{\delta_X}(X)\cup\bigcup_{w\in\partial X}B(w,2R_{X,n})\\
&=\mathscr{T}_{\delta_X}\left(X\setminus \bigcup_{w\in\partial X}B(w,R_{X,n})\right)\cup \bigcup_{w\in\partial X} B(w,2R_{X,n}).
\end{align*}

It remains to estimate $\operatorname{diam}\Gamma_n[x,y]$. The upper bound follows from Definition \ref{def:tile decomposition}(ii), Lemma \ref{lem:properties of thickening}(ii), and \eqref{eq:def of delta X}. For the lower bound, suppose that $W\in {\bf X}^n$ such that $\delta_W=\min_{Z\in {\bf X}^n, Z\cap X\neq\emptyset}\delta_Z$. Suppose also that $X$ is a leaf tile and that $v_x=v_y=v$. By (i) we have $\operatorname{diam} O\geq \operatorname{diam} X$. But $\operatorname{diam} O=\operatorname{diam} \partial O$ and thus Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(iii) gives
$$\operatorname{diam} \partial O\leq \operatorname{diam} \Gamma_n[x,y]+\operatorname{diam} \gamma_{v,n}^1+\operatorname{diam}\gamma_{v,n}^2\leq \operatorname{diam} \Gamma_n[x,y]+32CC_T\delta_X.$$
By \eqref{eq:def of delta X} we conclude that 
$$\operatorname{diam}\Gamma_n[x,y]\geq \operatorname{diam} X-32CC_T\delta_X\geq \delta_X.$$
Last, if $X$ is a branch tile, then $v_x\neq v_y$ and by Lemma \ref{lem:properties of BM}(vii), Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(iii), and \eqref{eq:def of delta X} we have
\[|x-y|\geq |v_x-v_{y}|-|v_{x}-x|-|v_{y}-y|\geq\frac{\operatorname{diam} X}{C^2}-32CC_{T}\delta_X\geq \delta_X. \qedhere\]
<!-- end proof-22 -->
<!-- lem-24: body_md -->
\label{lem:prerequisite for polygonal appr}
Let $n\in\mathbb{N}$, $x\in{\bf V}^n$, and let $X_{x,n+1}^{1}, X_{x,n+1}^{2}$ be the unique distinct $(n+1)$-tiles that contain $x$.   
\begin{enumerate}[(i)]
\item If $i\in\{1,2\}$ and $w_1,w_2\in D_{n+1}$ are the points on $D_{n+1}$ adjacent to $q_{x,{n+1}}^i$, then 
$$\gamma_{x,n}^i\cap \Gamma_{n+1}\subset \Gamma_{n+1}(w_1,q_{x,{n+1}}^i]\cup\Gamma_{n+1}[q_{x,{n+1}}^i,w_2).$$
In particular, if $y_1,y_2\in D_{n+1}\setminus\{q_{x,{n+1}}^i\}$ are adjacent, then
$$\operatorname{dist}(\gamma_{x,n}^i,\Gamma_{n+1}[y_1,y_2])\geq (2C)^{-1}\max\{\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^{1}},\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^{2}}\}.$$ 
\item Let $y\in {\bf V}^n$ and let $z,w\in D_{n+1}$ be points adjacent to $q_{y,{n+1}}^j,q_{x,{n+1}}^i$, respectively, where $i,j\in\{1,2\}$ are distinct if $x=y$. If $v_z,v_w$ are the associated $(n+1)$-vertices of $z,w$ respectively, then there exists $(n+1)$-tiles $X,Y$ such that $v_z,x\in \partial X$, $v_w,y\in \partial Y$ and 
$$\operatorname{dist}(\Gamma_{n+1}[z,q_{y,{n+1}}^j],\Gamma_{n+1}[w,q_{x,{n+1}}^i])\geq (2C)^{-1}\max\{\delta_{X},\delta_Y\}.$$
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lem-24 -->
<!-- proof-23: body_md -->
To show (i), fix $n\in\mathbb{N}$ and $x\in{\bf V}^n$. Let $w_{1},w_{2}$ be the adjacent points of $q_{x,n+1}^i$ for some $i\in\{1,2\}$. Fix $w\in \Gamma_{n+1}[y_1,y_2]$ for some $y_1,y_2\in D_{n+1}\setminus\{q_{x,n+1}^i\}$ adjacent and fix $z\in \gamma_{x,n}^i$ such that $|w-z|=\operatorname{dist}(\gamma_{x,n}^i,\Gamma_{n+1}[y_1,y_2])$. Note that 
\[ w\in \Gamma_{n+1}\setminus(\Gamma_{n+1}(w_1,q_{x,n+1}^i]\cup\Gamma_{n+1}[q_{x,n+1}^i,w_2)).\] 
Let also $v_{y_1},v_{y_2}$ be the associated $(n+1)$-vertices of $y_1,y_2$, respectively, and let $Y$ be an $(n+1)$-tile such that $v_{y_1},v_{y_2}\in\partial Y$, given from Lemma \ref{lem:diameter of the sides of W.S1}. By Lemma \ref{lem:properties of BM}(ii),(iii), $x\in {\bf V}^{n+1}$, and for $k\geq n$ there exist unique distinct $k$-tiles $X_{x,k}^{1},X_{x,k}^{2}$ that contain $x$. Let 
\[ R_{n+1}=2^{13}C^4C^2_{T}\min\{\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^1},\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^2}\}.\] 

We claim that $|w-z|\geq(2C)^{-1}\max\{\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^1},\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^2}\}$. Assuming the claim, since $\Gamma_{n+1}\subset \Omega_{n}$ from Lemma \ref{lem:def of thickening of Tm,eta} and $\gamma_{x,n}^i\subset \overline{\Omega_{n}}$ from Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(i) it follows that 
$$\gamma_{x,n}^i\cap \Gamma_{n+1}\subset \Gamma_{n+1}(w_1,q_{x,{n+1}}^i]\cup\Gamma_{n+1}[q_{x,{n+1}}^i,w_2).$$
To that end, we consider the following cases.

\emph{Case 1:} Suppose that $w\notin B(x,2R_{n+1})$. Note that $w\in\Omega_n$ from Lemma \ref{lem:def of thickening of Tm,eta} and $z\in \overline{\Omega_n}$ from Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(i). By Lemma \ref{lem:def of thickening of Tm,eta} there exists a $(2^6C_TC)$-bounded turning curve $\alpha\subset \overline{\Omega_n}$ that connects $w$ to $z$. Note that Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(iii) asserts that $z\in \gamma_{x,n}^i\subset B(x,2R_{n+1})$. Definition \ref{def:tile decomposition}(ii),(iv), the choice of $R_{n+1}$, and \eqref{eq:def of delta X} give
\begin{align*}
|w-z|&\geq (2^6CC_T)^{-1}\operatorname{diam} \alpha\\
&\geq (2^6CC_T)^{-1}\operatorname{dist}(\partial B(x,2R_{n+1}),\partial B(x,R_{n+1}))\\
&= (2^6CC_T)^{-1}R_{n+1}\\
&>\min\{\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^1},\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^2}\}.
\end{align*}

\emph{Case 2:} Suppose that $w\in B(x,2R_{n+1})$ and that $x\in\{v_{y_1},v_{y_2}\}$. Without loss of generality, we may assume that $x=v_{y_1}$ and hence $y_1=q_{x,n+1}^j$ for some $j\in\{1,2\}\setminus\{i\}$. By Lemma \ref{lem:properties of BM}(ii),(iii),(iv), we have that $Y\in \{X_{x,n+1}^1,X_{x,n+1}^2\}$ and that $Y$ is not a leaf tile. Hence, Lemma \ref{lem:diameter of the sides of W.S1} gives that $x\neq v_{y_2}$. Without loss of generality, suppose that $Y=X_{x,n+1}^1$.

Suppose now that $y_2\in\gamma_{v_{y_2},n+1}^{i'}$ for some $i'\in\{1,2\}$ and consider the following Jordan curve (see the proof of Lemma \ref{lem:diameter of the sides of W.S1})
$$\gamma_{v_{y_2},n+1}^{i'}\cup T[v_{y_2},x]\cup\gamma_{x,n+1}^j\cup\Gamma_{n+1}[q_{x,n+1}^j,y_2].$$
Denote by $U$ the domain enclosed by it. By Lemma \ref{lem:diameter of the sides of W.S}(i), $U\subset \Omega_{n+1}$ and by Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(i),(vi),(viii) and the fact that $y_2$ is adjacent to $q_{x,n+1}^j$ we have that $q_{x,n+1}^i\notin \overline{U}$. By Lemma \ref{lem:def of thickening of Tm,eta} there exists a $(2^6C_TC)$-bounded turning curve $\alpha\subset \overline{\Omega_{n+1}}$ connecting $w$ to $q_{x,n+1}^i$. If there exists $t\in\alpha$ such that $t\notin B(x,2R_{n+1})$, then Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(iii), the choice of $R_{n+1}$, and \eqref{eq:def of delta X} give
\begin{align*}
|w-z|&\geq |w-q_{x,n+1}^i|-|q_{x,n+1}^i-z|\\
&\geq (2^6C_TC)^{-1}\operatorname{diam}\alpha-\ell(\gamma_{x,n}^i)\\ 
&\geq (2^6C_TC)^{-1}R_{n+1}-16C_TC\min\{\delta_{X_{x,n}^1},\delta_{X_{x,n}^2}\}\\ 
&\geq 2^5C_TC\min\{\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^1},\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^2}\}-16C_TC\min\{\delta_{X_{x,n}^1},\delta_{X_{x,n}^2}\}\\ 
&\geq (2C)^{-1}\max\{\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^1},\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^2}\}.
\end{align*}
Hence, for the rest of this case we may assume that $\alpha\subset B(x,2R_{n+1})$. Observe also that $\gamma_{v_{y_2},n+1}^{i'}\cap B(x,2R_{n+1})=\emptyset$. To see this, let $z_1\in \gamma_{v_{y_2},n+1}^{i'}$ and $z_2\in B(x,2R_{n+1})$. Lemma \ref{lem:properties of BM}(vii), Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(iii), the choice of $R_{n+1}$, and \eqref{eq:def of delta X} give
\begin{align*}
|z_1-z_2|&\geq|v_{y_2}-x|- |z_1-v_{y_2}|-|z_2-x|\\
&\geq C^{-2}\operatorname{diam} X_{x,n+1}^1-16CC_T\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^1}-2R_{n+1}\\
&\ge (2C)^{-1}\max\{\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^1},\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^2}\}.
\end{align*}

Let $t$ be the first time that $\alpha $ meets $\partial U\setminus\Gamma_{n+1}[q_{x,n+1}^j,y_2]$ as we travel from $w$ to $q_{x,n+1}^i$. By the above observations, $t\in T[x,v_{y_2}]\cup\gamma_{x,n+1}^j$. Let 
\[p\in \partial B(x,2R_{n+1})\cap  T[v_{y_2},x]\quad \text{and}\quad q\in \partial B(x,2R_{n+1})\cap X_{x,n+1}^2\] 
such that $x\in T(p,q)\subset B(x,2R_{n+1})$. Denote by $W_1,W_2$ the connected components of $B(x,2R_{n+1})\setminus T[p,q]$. By Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(v), without loss of generality we may assume that $q_{x,n+1}^i\in W_i$ and $q_{x,n+1}^j\in W_j$. We may assume also that $w\in W_i$ since otherwise $[w,z]\cap T\neq\emptyset$ and the estimate of $|w-z|$ follows similarly as in the proof of Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(viii). If $t\in \gamma_{x,n+1}^j\setminus\{x\}$, then $\alpha[w,t]\subset W_j$ since $\alpha[w,t]\cap T[p,q]=\emptyset$, and $\gamma_{x,n+1}^j\subset W_j\cup \{x\}$, we reach a contradiction. If $t\in T(p,x]$, then by Lemma \ref{lem:Jordan domain prerequisite} there exists an arc $\zeta$ with one endpoint $p_1\in T[p,t)$ and another endpoint $p_2\in T[x,t)$ so that $\zeta\setminus\{p_1,p_2\}\subset U\cap W_i$. Since $q_{x,n}^i\notin \overline{U}$, let $\omega$ be an arc that connects $q_{x,n}^i$ to $t$ so that $\omega\setminus\{t\}\subset \mathbb{R}^2\setminus\overline{U}$. It follows that $\omega\cap (\zeta\setminus\{p_1,p_2\})\neq\emptyset$ which is contradiction since $\zeta\setminus\{p_1,p_2\}\subset U$, and $\omega\setminus\{t\}\subset \mathbb{R}^2\setminus\overline{U}$.
                                                                                                                                                                 

Suppose now that $t\in T(t_1,t_2)$ where $t_1,t_2\in T[p,v_{y_2})\cap\partial B(x,2R_{n+1})$ and $t\in T(t_1,t_2)\subset B(x,2R_{n+1})$. Denote by $V_1,V_2$ the connected components of $B(x,2R_{n+1})\setminus T[t_1,t_2]$. By Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(i), we have that $\gamma_{x,n}^i,\gamma_{x,n}^j\subset V_{i_1}$ for some $i_1\in \{1,2\}$. Similar arguments now with the above paragraph show that $w\in V_{i_2}$ for $i_2\in \{1,2\}\setminus\{i_1\}$. Thus, $[w,z]\cap T\neq\emptyset$, and the estimate of $|w-z|$ follows similarly as in the proof of Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(viii). See Figure \ref{fig:different connected components of a ball}.
\begin{figure}
    \centering
    \includegraphics[width=0.7\linewidth]{differentconnectedcomponentsofaball.png}
    \caption{Here the line segment $[w,z]$ intersects $T$.}
    \label{fig:different connected components of a ball}
\end{figure}


\emph{Case 3:} Suppose now that $w\in B(x,2R_{n+1})$ and that $x\notin\{v_{y_1},v_{y_2}\}$. Since $q_{x,n+1}^i\in \Gamma_{n+1}$, Lemma \ref{lem:def of thickening of Tm,eta} asserts the existence of a $(2^6C_TC)$-bounded turning curve $\alpha\subset \overline{\Omega_{n+1}}$ that connects $w$ to $q_{x,n+1}^i$. Similarly as in Case 2 we may assume that $\alpha\subset B(x,2R_{n+1})$.

For each $k\in\{1,2\}$ suppose that $y_k\in\gamma_{v_{y_k},n+1}^{i_k}$ where $i_1,i_2\in\{1,2\}$ are distinct if $v_{y_1}=v_{y_2}$. Denote by $O$ the Jordan domain enclosed by the curve
$$T[v_{y_1},v_{y_2}]\cup\gamma_{v_{y_1},{n+1}}^{i_1}\cup\Gamma_{n+1}[y_1,y_2]\cup \gamma_{v_{y_2},n+1}^{i_2}.$$
By Lemma \ref{lem:diameter of the sides of W.S}(i1) we have $O\subset \Omega_{n+1}$ and by Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(i),(viii), and the fact that $y_1$ is adjacent to $y_2$, we have $q_{x,n+1}^i\notin \overline{O}$.  

We claim that $\overline{B}(x,2R_{n+1})\cap \gamma_{v_{y_k},n+1}^{i_k}=\emptyset$ for each $k\in \{1,2\}$. Suppose first that $Y\cap (X_{x,n+1}^1\cup X_{x,n+1}^2)=\emptyset$. Without loss of generality, we may assume that $\delta_Y\leq \min\{\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^1},\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^2}\}$. By Definition \ref{def:tile decomposition}(iii), \eqref{eq:def of delta X}, and the choice of $R_{n+1}$,
\begin{align*}
\operatorname{dist}(\gamma_{v_{y_k,n+1}}^{i_k}, \overline{B}(x,2R_{n+1}))&\geq \operatorname{dist}(Y,X_{x,n+1}^1)-2R_{n+1}-16C_TC\delta_Y\\ 
&\ge C^{-1}\operatorname{diam} X_{x,n+1}^1-2R_{n+1}-16C_TC\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^1}>0.
\end{align*}
Suppose now that $Y\cap X_{x,n+1}^l\neq\emptyset$ for some $l\in \{1,2\}$. Traveling from $v_{y_k}$ to $x$ along $T[v_{y_k},x]$, let $u\in \partial X_{x,n+1}^l\cap T[v_{y_k},x)$. Definition \ref{def:tile decomposition}(ii), Lemma \ref{lem:properties of BM}(viii), \eqref{eq:def of delta X}, and the choice of $R_{n+1}$ give
\begin{align*}
\operatorname{dist}(\gamma_{v_{y_k,n+1}}^{i_k}, \overline{B}(x,2R_{n+1}))&\geq (C_T)^{-1}\operatorname{diam} T[x,v_{y_k}]-2R_{n+1}-16C_TC\delta_Y\\ 
&\geq (C_T)^{-1}|u-x|-2R_{n+1}-16C_TC\delta_Y\\ 
&\geq(C_TC^2)^{-1}\operatorname{diam} X_{x,n+1}^l-2R_{n+1}-32C_TC^2\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^l}>0.
\end{align*}
The proof of the claim is complete. 

It follows now with similar arguments as in Case 2 that $w,z$ are in different connected components of $B(x,2R_{n+1})\setminus T[t_1,t_2]$ for some $t_1,t_2\in \partial B(x,2R_{n+1})$ such that $T(t_1,t_2)\subset B(x,2R_{n+1})$. The estimate of $|w-z|$ follows similarly now as in the proof of Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(viii).

To show (ii), fix $n\in\mathbb{N}$, and $x,y\in{\bf V}^n$. Let $w,z\in D_{n+1}$ be adjacent points of $q_{x,{n+1}}^i,q_{y,{n+1}}^j$, respectively, where $i,j\in\{1,2\}$ are distinct if $x=y$. Moreover, let $v_z,v_w$ be the associated $(n+1)$-vertices of $z,w$ respectively. By Lemma \ref{lem:diameter of the sides of W.S1} there exist $(n+1)$-tiles $X,Y$ such that $v_z,x\in \partial X$ and $v_w,y\in \partial Y$. 

Suppose first that $x\neq y$. By Lemma \ref{lem:properties of BM}(ii),(iv) it follows that $X\cap Y=\emptyset$. Let $u_1\in \Gamma_{n+1}[w,q_{x,n+1}^i]$ and $u_2\in \Gamma_{n+1}[z,q_{y,n+1}^j]$. By Lemma \ref{lem:properties of thickening}(iii), Lemma \ref{lem:diameter of the sides of W.S}(iii), 
\[ u_1\in \mathscr{T}_{\delta_X}(X)\cup \bigcup_{v\in \partial X}B(v,2R_{X,n}), \quad u_2\in \mathscr{T}_{\delta_Y}(Y)\cup\bigcup_{v\in \partial Y}B(v,2R_{Y,n})\] 
where $R_{X,n},R_{Y,n}$ are as in the Lemma \ref{lem:diameter of the sides of W.S}(iii). If $u_1\in\mathscr{T}_{\delta_X}(X)$ and $u_2\in \mathscr{T}_{\delta_{Y}}(Y)$, then by Lemma \ref{lem:def of thickening of Tm,eta} $|u_1-u_2|\geq (2C)^{-1}\max\{\delta_X,\delta_Y\}$. Suppose now that $u_1\in B(v,2R_{X,n})$, and $u_2\in B(v',2R_{Y,n})$ for some $v\in \partial X$ and $v'\in \partial Y$. Assume as we may that $\delta_X\geq \delta_Y$. By Definition \ref{def:tile decomposition}(iii), \eqref{eq:def of delta X} and the choice of $R_{X,n},R_{Y,n}$ we have 
$$|u_1-u_2|\geq \operatorname{dist}(X,Y)-2R_{X,n}-2R_{Y,n}\geq\frac{\operatorname{diam} X}{C}-4R_{X,n}>\frac{\max\{\delta_X,\delta_Y\}}{2C}.$$
The rest of the cases follow similarly.

Suppose now that $x=y$. Then $i\neq j$, and $X\cap Y\neq\emptyset$. We claim that 
\[\Gamma_{n+1}[w,q_{x,n+1}^i]\cap\Gamma_{n+1}[z,q_{y,n+1}^j]=\emptyset.\] 
Assuming the claim, it follows from Lemma \ref{lem:properties of thickening}(iii), Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(iv), Lemma \ref{lem:diameter of the sides of W.S}(iii) that $$\Gamma_{n+1}[w,q_{x,n+1}^i]\subset \bigcup_{Z\in {\bf X}^{n+1}, Z\cap X\neq\emptyset}\mathscr{T}_{\delta_Z}(Z)$$
and 
$$\Gamma_{n+1}[w,q_{x,n+1}^i]\subset \bigcup_{W\in {\bf X}^{n+1}, W\cap Y\neq\emptyset}\mathscr{T}_{\delta_W}(W).$$
Hence, by Definition \ref{def:tile decomposition}(ii), \eqref{eq:def of delta X}, and the above facts we have
$$\operatorname{dist}(\Gamma_{n+1}[z,q_{y,{n+1}}^j],\Gamma_{n+1}[w,q_{x,{n+1}}^i])\geq \min_{Z\in {\bf X}^{n+1},Z\cap(X\cup Y)\neq\emptyset}\delta_Z\geq \frac{\max\{\delta_{X},\delta_Y\}}{2C}.$$
We show the claim. By Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(vi),(viii), and the fact that $\Gamma_{n+1}$ is a Jordan curve, it is enough to show that $w\neq z$. Suppose then that $w=z$. By Lemma \ref{lem:properties of BM}(vi) and Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(vii), the set
$$\gamma_{x,{n+1}}^i\cup\Gamma_{n+1}[q_{x,{n+1}}^i,w]\cup\Gamma_{n+1}[w,q_{x,{n+1}}^j]\cup\gamma_{x,{n+1}}^j$$
is a Jordan curve enclosing a Jordan domain $X\subset O\subset \Omega_{n+1}$. Therefore, if $\gamma_{v_w,n+1}^{k_0}$ is the arc that does not contain $w$, then Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(i) asserts that $\gamma_{v_w,n+1}^{k_0}\cap \partial O\neq\emptyset$. We reach a contradiction from Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(i),(viii) and the fact that the points $q_{x,{n+1}}^i,w$ and $q_{x,{n+1}}^j,w$ are adjacent.
<!-- end proof-23 -->
<!-- lem-25: body_md -->
\label{lem:polygonal curves}
     There exists a family of arcs
\begin{align*}
\{\zeta_{x,k}^i: x\in {\bf W}^*, k\geq \operatorname{\mathtt{L}}(x), i\in\{1,2\}\}
\end{align*} 
so that the following are true for all $x\in {\bf W}^*$, $k\geq \operatorname{\mathtt{L}}(x)$, $i\in\{1,2\}$, and for $X_{x,k}^1,X_{x,k}^2$ that are the unique distinct $k$-tiles that contain $x$.
\begin{enumerate}[(i)]
\item One endpoint of $\zeta_{x,k}^i$ is $x$, the other is on $\Gamma_k$, which we call $p_{x,k}^i$, the rest of the curve is in $\Omega_k\setminus T$, and if $l\geq k$, then $\zeta_{x,k}^i\cap \Gamma_l=\{p_{x,l}^i\}$.
\item If $l\ge k$, then $\zeta_{x,l}^i=\zeta_{x,k}^i[x,p_{x,l}^i]$. 
\item Let $0<r\leq \min\{\delta_{X_{x,k}^1},\delta_{X_{x,k}^2}\}$ and $p_{r},q_{r}\in\partial B(x,r)\cap T$ such that $x\in T(p_{r},q_{r})\subset B(x,r)$. Denote by $U_{x,r}^1,U_{x,r}^2$ the connected components of $B(x,r)\setminus T[p_{r},q_{r}]$. The connected component of $B(x,r)\cap \zeta_{x,k}^i$ that contains $x$ lies in $U_{x,r}^i\cup\{x\}$. 
\item We have $\zeta_{x,k}^i[p_{x,k}^i,p_{x,k+1}^i]\subset\mathcal{G}_{\lambda_{k}}$ where $\lambda_k=2^{-l_k}$ and $l_k\in\mathbb{N}$ is chosen such that $2^{-l_k}<(16C)^{-1}{\min\{\delta_{X_{x,k+1}^1},\delta_{X_{x,k+1}^2}\}}\leq 2^{-l_k+1}$.
\item We have $\operatorname{diam}\zeta_{x,k}^i[p_{x,k}^i,p_{x,k+1}^i]\simeq_{C_T,C}\min\{\delta_{X_{x,k+1}^1},\delta_{X_{x,k+1}^2}\}$. 
\item Suppose that $p_{y,k}^j$ is adjacent of $p_{x,k}^i$ in $\Gamma_k$ for some $y\in {\bf W}^k$ and $j\in \{1,2\}$. There exists $X\in {\bf X}^k$ such that $x,y\in \partial X$, $T(x,y)\subset X\setminus\partial X$, and $\Gamma_k[p_{x,k}^i,p_{y,k}^j]\subset \mathcal{G}_{\delta_Z}$ where $Z\in{\bf X}^n$ such that $\delta_Z=\min_{W\in{\bf X}^n,W\cap X\neq\emptyset}\delta_W$. Last, we have that $\operatorname{diam} \Gamma_k[p_{x,k}^i,p_{y,k}^j]\simeq_{C_T,C}\delta_Z$.
\item We have $\zeta_{x,k}^1\cap\zeta_{x,k}^2=\{x\}$.
\item Let $y\in {\bf W}^*\setminus\{x\}$, $l\geq \operatorname{\mathtt{L}}(y)$, $j\in\{1,2\}$, and $t=\max\{l+1,k+1\}$. Let also $Y_{y,t}^1,Y_{y,t}^2$ be the unique distinct $t$-tiles that contain $y$. Then $$\operatorname{dist}(\zeta_{x,k}^i,\zeta_{y,m}^j)\gtrsim_{C_T,C}\max\{ \delta_{X_{x,t}^1},\delta_{X_{x,t}^2}, \delta_{Y_{y,t}^1},\delta_{Y_{y,t}^2}\}.$$
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lem-25 -->
<!-- proof-24: body_md -->
Let $x\in {\bf W}^*$, $n=\operatorname{\mathtt{L}}(x)$, and $i\in \{1,2\}$. Also, for each $m\geq n$, let $X_{x,m}^1,X_{x,m}^2$ be the $m$-tiles such that $\{x\}=X_{x,m}^1\cap X_{x,m}^2$, given from Lemma \ref{lem:properties of BM}(ii),(iii). Recall the arc $\gamma_{x,n}^i$ from Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}. For each $k\geq n$ let $q_{x,k}^i$ be the endpoint of the arc $\gamma_{x,k}^i$ on $\Gamma_k$. By Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(ii) we have $\gamma_{x,n}^i=\bigcup_{k=n}^{\infty}\gamma_{x,k}^i[q_{x,k}^i,q_{x,k+1}^i]$. We replace each arc $\gamma_{x,k}^i[q_{x,k}^i,q_{x,k+1}^i]$ by a polygonal one satisfying the desired properties. We do this by induction on $k$. 
 
Suppose first that $k=n$. Let $w$ be the first time that the arc $\gamma_{x,n}^i$ meets $\Gamma_{{n+1}}$ as we travel from $q_{x,n}^i$ to $q_{x,n+1}^i$. By Lemma \ref{lem:def of thickening of Tm,eta} we have $O_n:=\Omega_{{n}}\setminus\overline{\Omega_{{n+1}}}\neq\emptyset$. Hence, Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(i) gives that $\gamma_{x,n}^i(q_{x,n}^i,w)\subset O_n$. See Figure \ref{fig:polygonal arcs step 1}.
\begin{figure}[H]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.5\linewidth]{polygonalarcsstep1.png}
\caption{}
\label{fig:polygonal arcs step 1}
\end{figure}


First, we replace the arc $\gamma_{x,n}^i[q_{x,n}^i,w]$. Let $\lambda_{n}$ as in the statement and consider the square grid $\mathcal{G}_{\lambda_{n}}$ and the associated family of (closed) squares $\Sigma_{\lambda_{n}}$. Let 
$$\mathcal{A}=\{S\in \Sigma_{\lambda_{n}}\cap\overline{O_n}:S\cap \gamma_{x,n}^i[q_{x,n}^i,w]\neq\emptyset\}.$$
Recall the scales $r_{x,l}$, $l\geq n$ from Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}. For simplicity, write $r_l$. We claim that 
\begin{align}\label{eq:local distance of Gn,Gn+1}
\operatorname{dist}(\Gamma_n\cap B(x,r_n),\Gamma_{n+1}\cap B(x,r_{n+1}))>4\lambda_{n}.
\end{align}
Assuming the claim, since by Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(iii), $\gamma_{x,n}^i\subset B(x,r_{x,n+1})\cup B(x,r_{x,n})$, it follows that $\mathcal{A}\neq\emptyset$. To show the claim, let $z_1\in \Gamma_{n+1}\cap B(x,r_{n+1})$ and $z_2\in \Gamma_n\cap B(x,r_n)$. By Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(iv), $z_1\in \mathscr{T}_{\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^j}}(X_{x,n+1}^j)$ and $z_2\in \mathscr{T}_{\delta_{X_{x,n}^{j'}}}(X_{x,n}^{j'})$ for some $j,j'\in \{1,2\}$. Lemma \ref{lem:properties of thickening}(ii) asserts the existence of $z\in X_j^{n+1}$ such that $|z_2-z|<8\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^j}$. Lemma \ref{lem:properties of thickening}(ii) and \eqref{eq:def of delta X} now give
$$|z_1-z_2|\geq |z_2-z|-|z_1-z|\geq \delta_{X_{x,n}^{j'}}-8\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^j}>4\lambda_{n}.$$
The proof of the claim is complete.

By Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(iv), $\Gamma_n\cap B(x,r_{n})\subset \mathcal{G}_{\min\{\delta_{X_{x,n}^1},\delta_{X_{x,n}^2}\}}$ and by \eqref{eq:def of delta X}, $\lambda_n$ divides $\min\{\delta_{X_{x,n}^1},\delta_{X_{x,n}^2}\}$. Thus, there exists $S_1\in\mathcal{A}$ such that $q_{x,n}^i\in \partial S_1$. Let $\ell_1$ be a side of $\partial S_1$ such that $\ell_1$ meets $\Gamma_n$ at a unique point, which we call $p_{x,n}^i$, and $\ell_1\setminus\{p_{x,n}^i\}\subset O_n$ from \eqref{eq:local distance of Gn,Gn+1}. Let $t\in\ell_1$ be the endpoint of $\ell_1$ on $O_n$. The point $p_{x,n}^i$ is the replacement of the point $q_{x,n}^i$. The fact that $\Gamma_{n+1}\cap B(x,r_{n+1})\subset \mathcal{G}_{\min\{\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^1},\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^2}\}}$ and that $\lambda_n$ divides $\min\{\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^1},\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^2}\}$ implies that there exists a square $S_2\in\mathcal{A}$ such that $w\in \partial S_2$. By \eqref{eq:local distance of Gn,Gn+1} there exists ${s}\in \partial S_2\cap O_n$ that is a corner of $S_2$. Since $\gamma_{x,n}^i(q_{x,n}^i,w)\subset O_n$ is connected we can find an arc $\omega\subset \bigcup_{S\in\mathcal{A}}\partial S\cap O_n$ that connects $t$ to $s$. See Figure \ref{fig:polygonal arcs step 2}.

\begin{figure}[H]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.7\linewidth]{polygonalarcsstep2.png}
\caption{}
\label{fig:polygonal arcs step 2}
\end{figure}


Set now 
$\omega_{x,n}^i=\ell_1\cup \omega$. Note that $\omega_{x,n}^i$ is an arc that connects $p_{x,n}^i$ to $s$. Since $\omega_{x,n}^i\subset \overline{O_n}$, it follows from Lemma \ref{lem:def of thickening of Tm,eta} that $\omega_{x,n}^i\cap T=\emptyset$. Note that if $z\in \gamma_{x,n}^i[q_{x,n}^i,w]$ and $z'\in \omega_{x,n}^i$, then $|z-z'|\leq 2\lambda_n$. Thus, by Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(viii)  we have $\omega_{x,n}^i\cap \{\gamma_{y,m}^1,\gamma_{y,m}^2\}=\emptyset$ for $y\in{\bf V}^m\setminus\{x\}$ and that $\omega_{x,n}^i\cap\gamma_{x,n}^j=\emptyset$ for $j\in\{1,2\}\setminus\{i\}$. Last, Lemma \ref{lem:properties of thickening}(ii), Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(iii), and \eqref{eq:def of delta X} give 
\begin{align*}
\operatorname{diam} \omega_{x,n}^i&\leq 4\lambda_n+ \operatorname{diam} \gamma_{x,n}^i[q_{x,n}^i,w]\\
&\leq 4\lambda_n+\ell(\gamma_{x,n}^i)\\ 
&\leq 4\lambda_n+64 C_ TC^3\min\{\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^1},\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^2}\}\\ 
&\lesssim_{C_T,C}\min\{\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^1},\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^2}\}.
\end{align*}

We replace now the arc $\gamma_{x,n}^i(w,q_{x,n+1}^i]$. Note that $q_{x,n+1}^i\in D_{n+1}$ and hence, denote by $z_{1},z_{2}$ its adjacent points in $\Gamma_{n+1}$. By Lemma \ref{lem:prerequisite for polygonal appr}(i) there exists $m\in\{1,2\}$ such that $w\in\Gamma_{n+1}[q_{x,n+1}^i,z_{m}]$. Denote by $\alpha$ the arc that lie in $\Gamma_n[z_m,q_{x,n+1}^i]$ that connects $w$ to $q_{x,n+1}^i$. Consider the following set
$$\mathcal{A}'=\{S\in\Sigma_{\lambda_n}\cap \overline{O_n}:S\cap \alpha\neq\emptyset\}.$$
Note that by \eqref{eq:local distance of Gn,Gn+1}, $S_2\in\mathcal{A}'$. Moreover, by \eqref{eq:def of delta X} and Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(iii),(iv) we have $\gamma_{x,n}^i\subset B(x,r_{n+1})$, $\Gamma_{n+1}\cap B(x,r_{n+1})\subset \mathcal{G}_{\min\{\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^1},\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^2}\}}$, and that $\lambda_n$ divides $\min\{\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^1},\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^2}\}$. Hence, there exists a square $S_3\in\mathcal{A}'$ such that $q_{x,n+1}^i\in \partial S_3$. Let $\ell_2$ be a side of $\partial S_3$ that meets $\Gamma_{n+1}$ at a unique point, which we call $p_{x,n+1}^i\in\Gamma_{n+1}\cap \partial S_3$, and the other endpoint is in $O_n$ from \eqref{eq:local distance of Gn,Gn+1}, say $t'$, and $\ell_{2}\setminus\{p_{x,n+1}^i\}\subset O_n$. Since $\alpha$ is connected we can find an arc $\omega'\subset \bigcup_{S\in \mathcal{A}'}\partial S\cap O_n$ that connects $s$ to $t'$. See Figure \ref{fig:polygonal arcs step 3}.

\begin{figure}[H]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.7\linewidth]{polygonalarcsstep3.png}
\caption{}
\label{fig:polygonal arcs step 3}
\end{figure}

Set now $\omega_{x,n+1}^i=\omega'\cup\ell_{2}$. Note that $\omega_{x,n+1}^i$ is an arc that connects $s$ to $p_{x,n+1}^i$. Since $\omega_{x,n+1}\subset \overline{O_n}$, it follows now from Lemma \ref{lem:def of thickening of Tm,eta} that $\omega_{x,n+1}^i\cap  T=\emptyset$. Note also that if $z\in \alpha$ and $z'\in \omega_{x,n+1}^i$, then $|z-z'|\leq 2\lambda_n$. Thus, by Lemma \ref{lem:prerequisite for polygonal appr}(i) we have $\omega_{x,n+1}^i\cap \{\gamma_{y,m}^1,\gamma_{y,m}^2\}=\emptyset$ for $y\in{\bf V}^m\setminus\{x\}$ and that $\omega_{x,n+1}^i\cap \gamma_{x,n}^j=\emptyset$ for $j\in\{1,2\}\setminus\{i\}$. Last, Lemma \ref{lem:diameter of the sides of W.S}(iv) and \eqref{eq:def of delta X} give
\begin{align*}
\operatorname{diam} \omega_{x,n+1}^i&\leq 4\lambda_n+\operatorname{diam} \alpha\\
&\leq 4\lambda_n+\operatorname{diam}\Gamma_{n+1}[q_{x,n+1},z_m]\\ 
&\lesssim_{C,C_T}\min\{\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^1},\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^2}\}.
\end{align*}

Let now $\zeta_n^i\subset\omega_{x,n}^i\cup\omega_{x,n+1}^i$ be an arc that connects $p_{x,n}^i$ to $p_{x,n+1}^i$. By construction, $\zeta_n^i\cap T=\emptyset$, $\zeta_n^i\subset\mathcal{G}_{\lambda_n}\cap \overline{\Omega_n}$, $\zeta_n^i\cap \Gamma_n=\{p_{x,n}^i\}$, $\zeta_n^i\cap\Gamma_{n+1}=\{p_{x,n+1}^i\}$, $\zeta_n^i\cap \{\gamma_{y,m}^j\}_{j\in\{1,2\}}=\emptyset$ for $y\in{\bf V}^m\setminus\{x\}$, $\zeta_{n}^i\cap\gamma_{x,n}^j=\emptyset$ for $j\in\{1,2\}\setminus\{i\}$, and $\operatorname{diam} \zeta_n^i\lesssim\min\{\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^1},\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^2}\}.$ For the lower bound, 
similar arguments with \eqref{eq:local distance of Gn,Gn+1} show that 
\[ |p_{x,n}^i-p_{x,n+1}^i|\gtrsim_{C_T,C}\min\{\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^1},\delta_{X_{x,n+1}^2}\}.\]

Suppose now that we have replaced the arc $\gamma_{x,k}^i[q_{x,k}^i,q_{x,k+1}^i]$, for $k\geq n$, with $\zeta_k^i[p_{x,k}^i,p_{x,k+1}^i]$. To replace the arc $\gamma_{x,k}^i[q_{x,k+1}^i,q_{x,k+2}^i]$ the process is similar with the basis step with the only difference that we replace the point $q_{x,k+1}^i$ with $p_{x,k+1}^i$.

For each $k\geq n$, set 
$$\zeta_{x,k}^i=\bigcup_{\ell=k}^{\infty}\zeta_{\ell}^i=\bigcup_{\ell=k}^{\infty}\zeta_{x,\ell}^i[p_{x,\ell}^i,p_{x,\ell+1}^i].$$
Note that $x\in\zeta_{x,n}^i$ since by Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(iii)
$$\operatorname{dist}(x,\zeta_{x,k}^i)\leq |x-p_{x,k}^i|\leq |x-q_{x,k}^i|+|q_{x,k}^i-p_{x,k}^i|\lesssim_{C_T,C}\min\{\delta_{X_{x,k+1}^1},\delta_{X_{x,k+1}^2}\}$$ 
and thus for $k\to \infty$, $\min\{\delta_{X_{x,k+1}^1},\delta_{X_{x,k+1}^2}\}\to 0$ from Lemma \ref{lem:properties of BM}(viii) and \eqref{eq:def of delta X}.

Claims (i),(ii),(iv),(v) follow from the construction.

Claims (iii),(vii) follow from the construction and Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(v),(vi).

We show claim (vi). The existence of $k$-tile $X$ follows similar arguments with Lemma \ref{lem:diameter of the sides of W.S}(i) where instead of Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(v), we use (iii). The fact that $T(x,y)\subset X\setminus\partial X$ follows from Lemma \ref{lem:properties of BM}(iv). The fact that $\Gamma_n[p_{x,n}^i,p_{y,n}^j]\subset \mathcal{G}_{\delta_Z}$ where $Z\in{\bf X}^n$ such that $\delta_Z=\min_{W\in{\bf X}^n,W\cap X\neq\emptyset}\delta_W$ and the estimate of $\operatorname{diam} \Gamma_n[p_{x,n}^i,p_{y,n}^j]$  follows from Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(iv), Lemma \ref{lem:diameter of the sides of W.S}(iv), and the fact that for each $n\in\mathbb{N}$, $z\in {\bf V}^n$, $|q_{x,n}^i-p_{x,n}^i|\leq 2\lambda_n$.

Finally, claim (viii) follows Lemma \ref{lem:arzela curves}(viii), Lemma \ref{lem:prerequisite for polygonal appr}(i),(ii), and the fact that for each $n\in\mathbb{N}$, $z\in {\bf V}^n$, $|q_{x,n}^i-p_{x,n}^i|\leq 2\lambda_n$.
<!-- end proof-24 -->
<!-- proof-25: body_md -->
Fix a tree $T\subset \mathbb{R}^2$ that is $C_T$-bounded turning and has a $C$-tile decomposition $({\bf X}^n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$. Let $(k_X)_{X\in{\bf X}^n}$, $(\delta_X)_{X\in{\bf X}^n}$ as in \eqref{eq:def of delta X} and let $({\bf V}^n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}_0}$ as in Definition \ref{def:tile decomposition}. Note that Definition \ref{def:tile decomposition}(iv) asserts that $k_X\simeq_{C}k_Y$ for any $X\in {\bf X}^n$ and $Y\in {\bf X}^{n+1}$ with $X\cap Y\neq\emptyset$. Let also $(\Omega_n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$ as in Lemma \ref{lem:def of thickening of Tm,eta} and set $\Gamma_n=\partial \Omega_n$. Note that by Lemma \ref{lem:def of thickening of Tm,eta}, $\Omega_1\supset\Omega_2\supset\cdots$. Let $K\in \mathbb{N}$ be the constant given from Definition \ref{def:tile decomposition}(i) and let $N\in\mathbb{N}$ such that $N>4K$. Note that $N$ depends only on $C$. 
                              
                                          
            
                                                                                                                                                 

We define the set $\mathscr{W}$ in an inductive fashion. To that end, let 
$$K_n=\{p_{x,n}^i:x\in {\bf V}^n, i\in \{1,2\}\},$$
where $p_{x,n}^i$ is as in Lemma \ref{lem:polygonal curves}(i).
Fix a leaf $1$-tile $X$ in $T$ and let $\{v\}=\partial X$. Without loss of generality let $p_1,\dots,p_{m_1}$ be a counterclockwise enumeration of the points in $K_1$ along $\Gamma_1$ with $p_1=p_{v,1}^1$ and $p_{2}=p_{v,1}^2$. By Definition \ref{def:tile decomposition}(i), Lemma \ref{lem:polygonal curves} and the definition of $N$ we have $m_1\in \{4,\dots, N\}$. The points $p_1,\dots,p_{m_1}$ induce a sequence on the double points of ${\bf V}^1$ and the arcs $(\zeta_{x,1}^i)_{x\in {\bf V}^1,i\in \{1,2\}}$, namely
\begin{align*}
x_1,x_2,\dots,x_{m_1}\qquad \text{and}\qquad \zeta_1,\zeta_2\dots,\zeta_{m_1}.
\end{align*}
where $x_1=x_2=v$, and $\zeta_1=\zeta_{v,1}^1$, $\zeta_2=\zeta_{v,1}^2$.

Set $\mathscr{W}_0=\{\varepsilon\}$ and $\mathscr{W}_1=\{1,\dots,m_1\}\subset \{1,\dots,N\}$. Suppose that we have defined $\mathscr{W}_n=\{w_1^n,\dots,w_{m_n}^n\}\subset \{1,\dots,N\}^{n}$ for some $n\in\mathbb{N}$. For simplicity, we write $w_i=w_i^n$. Let 
$$\mathscr{W}_{n+1}=\{w_11,\dots,w_1N_{w_1},w_21,\dots,w_{m_n}1,\dots, w_{m_n}N_{w_{m_n}}\}$$
where for each index $i\in \{1,\dots,m_n\}$ and each word $u\in \{1,\dots,N_{w_i}\}$, $p_{w_iu}$ is the unique endpoint of $\zeta_{w_i}|\overline{\Omega_{n+1}}$ on $\Gamma_{n+1}$ by Lemma \ref{lem:polygonal curves}(i). Set $x_{w_i1}=x_{w_i}$ and $\zeta_{w_i1}=\zeta_{w_i}|\overline{\Omega_{n+1}}$. 
Finally, set $\mathscr{W}=\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}_0}\mathscr{W}_n$ and note that for each $n\in\mathbb{N}$, $\operatorname{card}(\mathscr{W}_n)<\infty$.

Properties (ii.a) and (ii.c) follow from the design of $\mathscr{W}$. Property (ii.b) follows from Lemma \ref{lem:properties of BM}(ii),(iii). Property (ii.d) follows from Lemma \ref{lem:polygonal curves}(viii). Property (ii.e) follows the fact that for each $n\in\mathbb{N}$, $T=\bigcup_{X\in {\bf X}^n}X$. 

Property (iii) follows mainly from . Properties (iii.a) and (iii.b) follow from Lemma \ref{lem:polygonal curves}(i). Property (iii.c) follows from Lemma \ref{lem:polygonal curves}(ii),(iv),(v). Property (iii.e) follows from Lemma \ref{lem:polygonal curves}(viii). Last, property (iii.d) follows from the design of $\mathscr{W}$.

Property (v) follows from Lemma \ref{lem:polygonal curves}(iii),(vii)

It remains to show properties (i) and (iv). Property (i.b) follows from the design of $\mathscr{W}$. Property (iv.a) follows from  Lemma \ref{lem:properties of thickening}(ii) and similar arguments with Lemma \ref{lem:def of thickening of Tm,eta}(iii),(iv). For the rest of the proof we show properties (i.a) and (iv.b) simultaneously.

Fix $n\in\mathbb{N}$, $i\in \{1,\dots,m_n\}$ and $w_i\in\mathscr{W}_n$. We show that $N_{w_i}\in \{2,\dots,N\}$. Note that by design of $\mathscr{W}$, the points $p_{w_i},p_{w_{i+1}}$ are adjacent in $\Gamma_n$. By Lemma \ref{lem:polygonal curves}(i),(vii),(viii) the set
$$\zeta_{w_i}\cup T[x_{w_i},x_{w_{i+1}}]\cup \zeta_{w_{i+1}}\cup \Gamma_n[p_{w_{i+1}},p_{w_i}].$$
is a Jordan curve and let $O$ denote the Jordan domain enclosed by it. Using similar arguments as in the proof of Lemma \ref{lem:diameter of the sides of W.S}(i),(ii), it follows that there exists a unique $X\in{\bf X}^n$ such that $x_{w_i},x_{w_{i+1}}\in \partial X$, $O\subset \Omega_n$, and that $O\cap T\subset X\setminus\partial X$. Let $\gamma_1,\gamma_2$ be the two arcs in $\Gamma_{n+1}$ that connect $p_{w_i1}$ to $p_{w_{i+1}1}$, where $\gamma_1$ is the arc that contains the points $p_{w_i1},\dots,p_{w_iN_{w_i}},p_{w_{i+1}1}$. 

We claim that $\gamma_1(p_{w_i1},p_{w_{i+1}1})\subset O$. Assume otherwise. By Lemma \ref{lem:polygonal curves}(i) either $\gamma_2(p_{w_i1},p_{w_{i+1}1})\subset O$ or $O\cap (\gamma_1\cup\gamma_2)=\emptyset$. If the latter is true, then $(\overline{O}\setminus\{p_{w_i1},p_{w_{i+1}1}\})\cap \Gamma_{n+1}=\emptyset$ and since $\overline{O}\setminus\{p_{w_i1},p_{w_{i+1}1}\}$ is connected, and $x_{w_i},x_{w_{i+1}}\in\overline{O}$ we have that $\overline{O}\setminus\{p_{w_i1},p_{w_{i+1}1}\}\subset \Omega_{n+1}$ which is false since $p_{w_i},p_{w_{i+1}}\in\overline{O}\cap \Gamma_n$ and, therefore, by Lemma \ref{lem:def of thickening of Tm,eta} they cannot be interior points of $\Omega_{n+1}$. Suppose now that $\gamma_2(p_{w_i1},p_{w_{i+1}1})\subset O$. Note that by construction of $\gamma_2$, there exists $p_{v1}\in \gamma_2$ where $v\in\mathscr{W}_n\setminus\{w_i,w_{i+1}\}$. By Lemma \ref{lem:properties of BM}(iv) and Lemma \ref{lem:polygonal curves}(i),(vii),(viii) it follows that $\zeta_{v1}\subset O$. In particular, since $O\cap T\subset X\setminus\partial X$, we have that $x_{v1}\in X$. But by (ii), $x_{v1}=x_v$ which implies that $x_v\in \partial X\cup Z$ for some $Z\in{\bf X}^n\setminus\{X\}$. We reach a contradiction. The proof of the claim is complete

Now by Lemma \ref{lem:properties of BM}(ii),(iv) and Lemma \ref{lem:polygonal curves}(i),(iii),(viii), $N_{w_i}\geq 2$. Since, 
\[ x_{w_i2},\dots,x_{w_iN_{w_i}}\in X\cap {\bf V}^{n+1},\] 
Definition \ref{def:tile decomposition}(i) gives that $N_{w_i}\leq N$ and property (i.b) follows. Last, property (iv.a) follows from the above work and Lemma \ref{lem:polygonal curves}(vi).
<!-- end proof-25 -->
<!-- prop-4: body_md -->
\label{prop:combinatorial connection T, T^W^T}
Let $T$ be a planar quasiconformal tree and let $f:T\to T'$ be a quasisymmetric branch-orientation-preserving homeomorphism. Let also, $({\bf X}^n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$ be a $C$-tile decomposition of $T$ and $\{\mathbb{X}^n\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$ be the $C'$-tile decomposition given from Proposition \ref{prop:tile-decomposition}. If $\mathscr{W}$, $\mathscr{W}'$ are the sets given from Proposition \ref{prop:polygonal curves} for $(T,({\textbf X}^n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}})$ and $(T',({\mathbb X}^n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}})$, respectively, then $\mathscr{W}=\mathscr{W}'$.
<!-- end prop-4 -->
<!-- proof-26: body_md -->
Applying Proposition \ref{prop:polygonal curves}, to $(T,({\bf X}^n))$ and $(T',({\mathbb X}^n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}})$ there exist integers $N,N'\geq 1$ and sets  $\mathscr{W}\subset\{1,\dots,N\}^*$, $\mathscr{W}'\subset \{1,\dots,N'\}$ satisfying the properties of Proposition \ref{prop:polygonal curves}. Since $g$ is a homeomorphism it follows that $N=N'$ and that for each $n\in\mathbb{N}$, $\operatorname{card}(\mathscr{W}_n)=\operatorname{card}(\mathscr{W}_n')$. It remains to show that $\mathscr{W}=\mathscr{W}'$. To this end, we claim that if $n\in\mathbb{N}$, if $w\in\mathscr{W}_n'$, and if $y_w\in g({\bf V}^n)$, then $y_w=g(x_w)$, i.e., $w\in \mathscr{W}_n$. Assuming the claim, it follows that $\mathscr{W}'_n\subset\mathscr{W}_n$. Similar arguments applied to $g^{-1}$ show that $\mathscr{W}_n\subset \mathscr{W}_n'$. For simplicity, we show the claim for $n=1$ and identical arguments work for any $n\in\mathbb{N}$. 

Let $\Gamma_1$ be the Jordan curve associated with $T$ from \textsection\ref{sec:sqthick}, and let $(\zeta_i)_{i\in\mathscr{W}_1}$ denote the arcs with endpoints $x_i$ and $p_i\in\Gamma_1$ as in Proposition \ref{prop:polygonal curves}. As in the proof of Proposition \ref{prop:polygonal curves}, we may assume that $x_1=x_2\in X$, where $X\in{\bf X}^1$ is a fixed leaf tile of $T$, and that the points $\{p_i\}_{i\in\mathscr{W}_1}$ are enumerated in counterclockwise order along $\Gamma_1$. Let also $(g(x_{i_j}))_{j\in\mathscr{W}_1}\subset g(\mathbf{V}^n)$, $i_j\in\mathscr{W}_1$, denote the double points of $T'$, let $\Gamma_1'$ be the Jordan curve associated with $T'$ from \textsection\ref{sec:sqthick}, and let $(\xi_j)_{j\in\mathscr{W}_1}$ denote the corresponding arcs with endpoints $g(x_{i_j})$ and $q_j\in \Gamma_1'$ as in Proposition \ref{prop:polygonal curves}. Suppose also that the points $\{q_j\}_{j\in\mathscr{W}_1}$ are likewise enumerated in counterclockwise order along $\Gamma_1’$. After relabeling, we may assume that 
\[ g(x_{i_1})=g(x_1)=g(x_2)=g(x_{i_2}).\] 
Finally, after a cyclic permutation of the indexing, we may further assume that $i_3\neq 3$; in particular, $i_3\notin\{1,2,3\}$.

Denote by $\gamma[p_2,p_{i_3}]\subset \Gamma_1$ the counterclockwise arc in $\Gamma_1$ that connects $p_2$ to $p_{i_3}$. Consider the following sets
\begin{align*}
    \gamma[p_2,p_{i_3}]\cup \zeta_{i_3}\cup T[x_{i_3},x_2]\cup \zeta_2,\qquad \Gamma_1'[q_2,q_{i_3}]\cup\xi_{i_3}\cup \mathcal{T}^{\mathcal{W}}[g(x_{i_3}),g(x_2)]\cup \xi_2.
\end{align*}
By Proposition \ref{prop:polygonal curves}(iv),(vii) both of these sets are Jordan curves. Denote by $O,O'$ the domains enclosed by them, respectively.

By Proposition \ref{prop:polygonal curves}(ii), $x_2\notin \{x_3,x_{i_3}\}$ and since $g$ is a homeomorphism $g(x_2)\notin\{g(x_3), g(x_{i_3})\}$. By construction of $\gamma$, $p_3\in \gamma(p_2,p_{i_3})$ and $p_1\notin \gamma$. By Proposition \ref{prop:polygonal curves}(i), $O\cap \Gamma_1=\emptyset$ and $O$ is connected with $\partial O\cap T\neq\emptyset$. Thus, by Proposition \ref{prop:polygonal curves}(iii), $O\subset \Omega_1$ and similarly, $O\subset \Omega_1'$. Finally, by Proposition \ref{prop:polygonal curves}(iv),(vii) and the fact that $O\subset \Omega_1$, we have that $\zeta_3\setminus\{x_3,p_3\}\subset O$ and that $\xi_3\setminus\{g(x_3)\}\subset \mathbb{R}^2\setminus\overline{O'}$. Hence, either $x_3\in T(x_2,x_{i_3})$ or $x_3\in O$.

Suppose first, that $x_3\in T(x_2,x_{i_3})$. Since $g$ is homeomorphism we have $g(x_3)\in T'(g(x_2),g(x_{i_3}))$. However, since $q_2,q_{i_3}$ are adjacent points in $\Gamma_1'$, Proposition \ref{prop:polygonal curves}(v) asserts the existence of $Y\in g({\bf X}^1)$ such that $g(x_2),g(x_{i_3})\in \partial Y$ and it asserts that $T'(g(x_2),g(x_{i_3}))\subset Y\setminus\partial Y$ which is a contradiction. 

Suppose now that $x_3\in O$. Traveling along $T[x_2,x_3]$ from $x_3$ to $x_2$, let $b\in T[x_2,x_3]$ be the first time that we meet $T[x_2,x_3]$. We claim that $b\in T(x_2,x_{i_3})$. Suppose otherwise that $b\in\{x_2,x_{i_3}\}$. Since $p_2,p_3$ are adjacent, by Proposition \ref{prop:polygonal curves}(v) there exists $Z\in{\bf X}^1$ such that $x_3,x_2\in \partial Z$ and $T(x_2,x_3)\subset Z\setminus\partial Z$. Thus, $b\neq x_{i_3}$. If $b=x_2$, then we claim that $\zeta_1\setminus\{x_2,p_1\}\subset O$. Assuming the claim, it follows from Proposition \ref{prop:polygonal curves}(iv),(vii) that $p_1\in \gamma(p_2,p_{i_3})$ which is contradiction. To show the claim, suppose otherwise. By Proposition \ref{prop:polygonal curves}(iv),(vii) we may assume that $\zeta_1\setminus\{x_2\}\subset \mathbb{R}^2\setminus\overline{O}$. Consider the following Jordan curve
$$\zeta_{i_3}\cup T[x_{i_3},x_2]\cup T[x_2,x_3]\cup \zeta_3\cup (\gamma[p_2,p_{i_3}]\setminus\Gamma_1[p_2,p_3))$$
and denote by $V$ the domain enclosed. Note that $V\subset O$. Let $B=B(x_2,r)$ where $r=\frac12\min_{W\in{\bf X}^1}\delta_W$. It is easy to verify that $x_3,x_{i_3},p_2,p_3\notin B$. Let $t_1\in \partial B\cap T(x_2,x_{i_3})$ and $t_2\in \partial B\cap T(x_2,x_{3})$ such that $x_2\in T(t_1,t_2)\subset B$. Denote by $W_1,W_2$ the connected components of $B\setminus T(t_1,t_2)$. By Lemma \ref{lem:Jordan domain prerequisite} there exists an arc $\alpha$ with one endpoint $t_1'\in T(x_2,t_1)$ and the other $t_2'\in T(x_2,t_2)$ such that $\alpha\setminus\{t_1',t_2'\}\subset V\cap W_i\subset O\cap W_i$ for some $i\in \{1,2\}$. But then, by Proposition \ref{prop:polygonal curves}(vii) we have that $(\alpha\setminus\{t_1',t_2'\})\cap (\zeta_1\cup \zeta_2)\neq\emptyset$ which is contradiction since $\zeta_2\subset \partial O$ and $\zeta_1\setminus\{x_2\}\subset \mathbb{R}^2\setminus\overline{O}$. See also Figure \ref{fig:combinatorial-intersection 1}. This proves the claim, and we get that $b\in T(x_2,x_3)$.

\begin{figure}
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.6\linewidth]{Combinatorialintersection1.png}
\caption{Here $\zeta_1,\zeta_2$ are in the same connected component of $B(x_2,r)\setminus T[t_1,t_2]$.}\label{fig:combinatorial-intersection 1}
\end{figure}


By Lemma \ref{lem: metric trees properties of BM}(ii) it follows that $b$ is a branch point and, therefore, $g(b)\in T'(g(x_{i_3}),g(x_2))$ is also
a branch point. By Lemma \ref{lem:Jordan domain prerequisite}, there exist arcs
$\sigma_1,\sigma_2,\omega_1,\omega_2$ such that
\begin{enumerate}
\item $\sigma_1$ and $\sigma_2$ have common endpoints $s_1\in T(b,x_2)$ and $s_2\in T(b,x_{i_3})$, whereas $\omega_1$ and $\omega_2$ have common endpoints 
\[ s_1'\in T'(g(b),g(x_2)) \quad\text{and}\quad s_2'\in T'(g(b),g(x_{i_3})),\]
\item the interiors (with the induced topology) of $\sigma_1$ and $\omega_1$ 
\[ \sigma_1\setminus\{s_1,s_2\}\subset O, \qquad \omega_1\setminus\{s_1',s_2'\}\subset O'.\]
\item the interiors (with the induced topology) of $\sigma_2$ and $\omega_2$
\[ \sigma_2\setminus\{s_1,s_2\}\subset\mathbb{R}^2\setminus\overline{O}, \qquad \omega_2\setminus\{s_1',s_2'\}\subset\mathbb{R}^2\setminus\overline{O'}.\]
\end{enumerate}

Denote by $D$, resp.\ $D'$, the domain enclosed by $\sigma_1\cup\sigma_2$, resp.\ $\omega_1\cup\omega_2$. Let $B_1,\ldots,B_k$ be the branches of $T$ at $b$. Then $g(B_1),\ldots,g(B_k)$ are the branches of $T'$ at $g(b)$. For each $i\in\{1,\ldots,k\}$, we have $\partial D\cap B_i\neq\emptyset$ and $\partial D'\cap g(B_i)\neq\emptyset$. Moreover, by Lemma \ref{lem: maximal arcs}(i), there exist $y_i\in\partial D\cap B_i$ and $z_i\in\partial D'\cap g(B_i)$ such that $T[b,y_i)\subset D$ and $T'[g(b),z_i)\subset D'$. Relabeling the branches, we may assume that $x_2\in B_1$, $x_{i_3}\in B_2$, and $x_3\in B_3$. Without loss of generality, we may choose $s_1=y_1$, $s_2=y_2$, $s_1'=z_1$, and $s_2'=z_2$. By Proposition \ref{prop:polygonal curves}(iii), the fact that $T$ is a tree, and that $x_3\in B_3$, it follows that $B_3\subset O$. Hence, since $y_3\in B_3$, we have that $y_3\in O$. Similarly, since $g(x_3)\notin\overline{O'}$, we have $z_3\notin\overline{O'}$.

Consider now the Jordan curves. 
\begin{align*}
\alpha_1 &=T[p_2,p_{i_3}]\cup\zeta_{i_3}\cup T[x_{i_3},y_2] \cup\sigma_1\cup T[y_1,x_2]\cup\zeta_2,\\
\alpha_2 &=T'[q_2,q_{i_3}]\cup\xi_{i_3}\cup T'[g(x_{i_3}),z_2] \cup\omega_1\cup T'[z_1,g(x_2)]\cup\xi_2.
\end{align*}
Denote by $U,U'$ the domains enclosed by $\alpha_1,\alpha_2$, respectively. Since $y_3\in O\cap\partial U$ and $\sigma_1\setminus\{y_1,y_2\}\subset O\cap\partial U$, the counterclockwise order of the points $\{y_i\}_{i=1}^{\operatorname{Val}(b)}$ along $\partial D$, starting from $y_2$, is
\[ y_2,\dots, y_3,\dots y_1,\dots .\]
On the other hand, since $z_3\notin\omega_1$ and $\omega_1\setminus\{z_1,z_2\}\subset O'\cap\partial U'$, their corresponding points occur counterclockwise along $\partial D'$, starting from $z_2$, in the order
\[z_2,\dots, z_1,\dots, z_3,\dots\]
This contradicts the assumption that $g$ is branch-orientation-preserving.
<!-- end proof-26 -->
<!-- thm-6: body_md -->
\label{thm: intro 2 quant.}
Let $T\subset\mathbb R^2$ be planar quasiconformal tree and let $g:T\to \mathbb{R}^2$ be a branch-orientation-preserving embedding.
\begin{enumerate}[(i)]
\item If $g$ is $\eta$-quasisymmetric, then $g$ extends to a $K$-quasiconformal homeomorphism $ F:\mathbb R^2\to\mathbb R^2$, with $K$ depending only on the bounded turning constant of $T$ and on $\eta$.
\item If $g$ is $L$-bi-Lipschitz, then $g$ extends to an $L'$-bi-Lipschitz homeomorphism $F:\mathbb R^2\to\mathbb R^2,$ with $L'$ depending only on the bounded turning constant of $T$ and on $L$.
\end{enumerate}
Conversely, if $F:\mathbb{R}^2\to\mathbb{R}^2$ is an orientation preserving homeomorphism and if $T\subset \mathbb{R}^2$ is a tree, then $F|T$ is branch-orientation-preserving.
<!-- end thm-6 -->
<!-- proof-27: body_md -->
The converse part follows directly by definition. 

Let $T,$ and $g$ be as in (i) and let $T'=g(T)$. By Proposition \ref{prop:tile-decomposition} there exist a $C_1$-tile decomposition $({\bf X}^n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$ of $T$ and a $C_2$-tile decomposition $({\mathbb X}^n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}$ of $T'$ where $C_1$ depends on the bounded turning constant of $T$ and $C_2$ depends on the same constant and the distortion $\eta$. Moreover, by Propositions \ref{prop:polygonal curves}, \ref{prop:combinatorial connection T, T^W^T} both $T$ and $T'$ induce the same set $\mathscr{W}\subset \{1,\dots,N\}^*$ where $N\in\mathbb{N}$ depends only on $C_1$. For each $n\in\mathbb{N}$ denote by $\Gamma_n$, resp. $\Gamma_n'$, the associated Jordan curve of $T$, resp. of $T'$, given from Proposition \ref{prop:polygonal curves}(iv). Last, by Proposition \ref{prop:polygonal curves}(iii),(iv), let $Z_w\in\mathbf{X}^n$ and $l_w$ be associated with the tree $T$, and $W_w\in{\mathbb X}^n$ and $l_w'$ be associated with the tree $T'$, respectively.

Fix now $n\in\mathbb{N}$ and let $\mathscr{W}_n=\{w_1^n,\dots,w_{m_n}^n\}$. For simplicity, we write $w_i^n = w_i$. By Proposition \ref{prop:polygonal curves}(iii),(iv),(v), there exist Jordan domains $D_{w_i}$ and $\Delta_{w_i}$ such that 
\begin{align*}
\partial D_{w_i}&= e_{w_i}^U\cup e_{w_i}^L \cup \bigcup_{j=1}^{N_{w_i}} e_{w_i}^{B_j} \cup e_{w_i}^R, \qquad \partial \Delta_{w_i} = f_{w_i}^U\cup f_{w_i}^L\cup \bigcup_{j=1}^{N_{w_i}} f_{w_i}^{B_j} \cup f_{w_i}^R.
\end{align*}
so that
\begin{enumerate}
\item the upper edges satisfy 
\begin{align*}
e_{w_i}^U=\Gamma_n[p_{w_i},p_{w_{i+1}}] \subset\mathcal G_{\delta_{Z_{w_i}}} \quad&\text{and}\quad \operatorname{diam} e_{w_i}^U\simeq_{C_1} \delta_{Z_{w_i}}\\
f_{w_i}^U=\Gamma_n'[q_{w_i},q_{w_{i+1}}] \subset G_{\delta_{W_{w_i}}} \quad&\text{and}\quad \operatorname{diam} f_{w_i}^U\simeq_{C_2} \delta_{W_{w_i}},
\end{align*}
\item the left edges satisfy
\begin{align*}
e_{w_i}^L=\zeta_{w_{i+1}}\setminus \zeta_{w_{i+1}1}(p_{w_{i+1}1},x_{w_{i+1}1}] \subset \mathcal{G}_{l_{w_{i+1}}} \quad&\text{and}\quad \operatorname{diam} e_{w_i}^L\simeq_{C_1} l_{w_{i+1}}\\
f_{w_i}^L=\xi_{w_{i+1}}\setminus\xi_{w_{i+1}1}(q_{w_{i+1}1},g(x_{w_{i+1}1})] \subset \mathcal{G}_{l_{w_{i+1}}'} \quad&\text{and}\quad \operatorname{diam} f_{w_i}^L\simeq_{C_2} l_{w_{i+1}}',
\end{align*}
\item
the bottom edges satisfy
\begin{align*}
e_{w_i}^{B_j} = \Gamma_{n+1}[p_{w_ij},p_{w_i(j+1)}] \subset \mathcal{G}_{\delta_{Z_{w_ij}}} \quad&\text{and}\quad \operatorname{diam} e_{w_i}^{B_j}\simeq_{C_1}\delta_{\delta_{Z_{w_ij}}}\\
f_{w_i}^{B_j} = \Gamma_{n+1}'[q_{w_ij},q_{w_i(j+1)}] \subset\mathcal G_{\delta_{W_{w_ij}}} \quad&\text{and}\quad \operatorname{diam} f_{w_i}^{B_j}\simeq_{C_2} \delta_{W_{w_ij}},
\end{align*}
with $\lambda_{w_i}^{B_j}=\delta_{Z_{w_ij}}$ and $\ell_{w_i}^{B_j}=\delta_{W_{w_ij}}$
\item
the right edges satisfy
\begin{align*}
e_{w_i}^R = \zeta_{w_i} \setminus \zeta_{w_i1}(p_{w_i},x_{w_i1}] \subset \mathcal{G}_{l_{w_i}} \quad&\text{and}\quad \operatorname{diam} e_{w_i}^R\simeq_{C_1}l_{w_i}\\
f_{w_i}^R= \xi_{w_i} \setminus \xi_{w_i1}(q_{w_i},g(x_{w_i1})] \subset\mathcal G_{l_{w_i}'}\quad&\text{and}\quad \operatorname{diam} f_{w_i}^R\simeq_{C_2}l_{w_i}'.
\end{align*}
\end{enumerate} 

\begin{figure}[ht]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.6\linewidth]{whitneytile.png}
\caption{A Whitney tile $D_w$.}
\label{fig:whitneytile}
\end{figure}

Recalling that $N_{w_i}\leq N$ and that
\begin{align*}
\delta_{Z_{w_i}} \simeq_{C_1} l_{w_{i+1}} \simeq_{C_1} \delta_{Z_{w_ij}} \simeq_{C_1} l_{w_i}\\
\delta_{W_{w_i}} \simeq_{C_2} l_{w_{i+1}}' \simeq_{C_2} \delta_{W_{w_ij}} \simeq_{C_2} l_{w_i}'
\end{align*}
it follows that there exists $K\in\mathbb{N}$ depending on $C_1,C_2$ such that all edges above consist of at most $K$ line segments.

Consider now, for each $A\in\{U,L,R,B_1,\ldots,B_{N_{w_i}}\}$, the normalized arclength parametrizations
$G_{w_i}^A:e_{w_i}^A\to[0,1]$ and $H_{w_i}^A:f_{w_i}^A\to[0,1]$, with corresponding endpoints mapped to $0$ and $1$, and set $h_{w_i}^A=(H_{w_i}^A)^{-1}\circ G_{w_i}^A$. By the finite-model construction, the source and target edges, after normalization by similarities, belong to finite families of nondegenerate polygonal arcs determined by $C_1$ and $C_2$. Normalized arclength parametrizations commute with these similarities, so the corresponding normalized edge maps also belong to a finite family. Each of these maps is bi-Lipschitz. Together with the diameter estimates above, this gives a constant $L\ge1$, depending only on $C_1$ and $C_2$, such that every $h_{w_i}^A$ is a $(\delta_{W_{w_i}}/\delta_{Z_{w_i}},L)$-quasi-similarity.

Define $h':\Gamma_1\to \Gamma_1'$ by $h'|e^U_{v}=h_v^{U}$ for each $v\in\mathscr{W}_1$ and for each $w\in \mathscr{W}$ define $\alpha_{w}:\partial D_w \to \partial \Delta_w$ by $\alpha_{w}|e_{w}^A=h_{w}^A$. Applying Lemma \ref{lem:Tukia}, we can find extension $\alpha_{w}':\overline{D_{w}}\to \overline{\Delta_{w}}$ that is $(\frac{\delta_{Z_w}}{\delta_{W_w}},L')$-quasi-similarity, and we can find extension $H':\mathbb{R}^2\setminus\Omega_1\to\mathbb{R}^2\setminus \Omega'_1$ that is $(\frac{\delta_{Z_v}}{\delta_{W_v}},M')$-quasi-similarity, with $L',M'$ depending only on $L$. Let now $F:\mathbb{R}^2\to\mathbb{R}^2$ with
\[ F|_{\mathbb{R}^2\setminus\Omega_1}=H', \quad F|_{\overline{D_w}}=\alpha'_{w} \text{ for each $w\in \mathscr{W}$,} \quad F|_{T}=g.\]

First, we claim that $F|_{\mathbb{R}^2\setminus T}$ is an $M$-quasiconformal homeomorphism, where $M\geq 1$ depends only on $C_1,C_2$. By construction, $F|_{\mathbb{R}^2\setminus T}$ is a homeomorphism. Let $x\in\mathbb{R}^2\setminus T$. If $x$ belongs to the interior of a Whitney domain $D_w$, then, for all sufficiently small $r>0$, we have $B(x,r)\subset D_w$. Since $F|_{D_w}=\alpha'_w$ is an $(s_w,L')$-quasi-similarity for some $s_w>0$, it follows directly from the definition of quasiconformal distortion $H_F$ that $ H_F(x)\leq (L')^2$. The same argument applies if $x\in \mathbb{R}^2\setminus\overline{\Omega}_1$, with $M'$ in place of $L'$.

It remains to consider the case where $x$ belongs to the boundaries of Whitney domains. By construction, there are at most 4 such domains meeting at $x$, and their scales are mutually comparable. The corresponding scaling factors of the quasi-similarities $F|_{D_w}$ are therefore mutually comparable, with constants depending only on $C_1,C_2$. Applying the quasi-similarity inequalities on each of the domains containing $x$ gives $H_F(x)\leq M$, where $M$ depends only on $C_1,C_2$. Consequently, $F|_{\mathbb{R}^2\setminus T}$ is $M$-quasiconformal.

It remains to show that $F$ is a homeomorphism. Assuming the claim, it follows from Lemma \ref{lem: Nakki vaisalla} and Lemma \ref{lem:qc removable}, that $F$ is a $\Lambda$-quasiconformal map with $F(T)=g(T)=T'$, where $\Lambda$ depends only on $C_1,C_2$.

Suppose that $x_n\to x\in T$. Passing to a subsequence we may assume that there exists an increasing sequence $j_n$ such that $x_n\in \overline{D_{w_{j_n}}}$ for some $w_{j_n}\in \mathscr{W}_{j_n}$. Let $z_n=p_{w_{j_n}}$ and let $v_n=x_{w_{j_n}}$. By Proposition \ref{prop:polygonal curves}, $\operatorname{diam} \overline{D_{w_{j_n}}},\operatorname{diam} \overline{\Delta_{w_{j_n}}}\to 0$. By Proposition \ref{prop:polygonal curves},
$$|v_n-x|\leq |v_n-z_n|+|z_n-x_n|+|x_n-x|\lesssim \delta_{X_{j_n}}+\operatorname{diam} \overline{D_{w_{j_n}}}+|x_n-x|\to 0,$$
where $X_{j_n}\in\mathbf{X}^{j_n}$ contain $v_n$ on their boundary. Hence, $v_n\to x$. By continuity of $g$ we have that $g(v_n)\to g(x)$. Note also that $F(x_n)\in\overline{\Delta_{w_{j_n}}}$, $F(z_n)=q_{w_{j_n}}$, and $F(v_n)=g(x_{w_{j_n}})$. Therefore, by Proposition \ref{prop:polygonal curves},
\begin{align*}
|F(x_n)-F(x)| &\leq |F(x_n)-F(z_n)|+|F(z_n)-F(v_n)|+|F(v_n)-F(x)| \\ & \lesssim \operatorname{diam} \overline{\Delta_{w_{j_n}}}+\delta_{Y_{j_n}}+|g(v_n)-g(x)|\to 0,
\end{align*}
where $Y_{j_n}\in{\mathbb X}^{j_n}$ contain $g(v_n)$ on their boundary. It follows that $F(x_n)\to F(x)$ which means that $F$ is continuous. Identical arguments show that $F^{-1}$ is also continuous. Thus, $F$ is a homeomoprhism.

To show (ii), suppose that $g:T\to T'$ is $L$-bi-Lipschitz. In this case the maps $h_{w_i}^A$ are uniformly bi-Lipschitz, with constants depending only on the bounded turning constant of $T$ and on $L$. Consequently, the extensions constructed above may be chosen so that the restrictions of $F$ to the Whitney polygons are uniformly bi-Lipschitz. Thus there exists $L'\geq1$, depending only on the bounded turning constants of $T,T'$ and on $L$, such that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:bilip-pieces}
(L')^{-1}\ell(\gamma) \leq \ell(F\circ\gamma)\leq L'\ell(\gamma)
\end{equation}
whenever $\gamma$ is a rectifiable arc contained either in $T$ or in one of the Whitney domains.

We claim that $F$ is $L'$-BLD. Let $\gamma:[a,b]\to\mathbb R^2$ be a rectifiable path, which we may assume to be parametrized by arclength. Since $T$ and the boundaries of the Whitney domains form a closed set, the portions of $\gamma$ contained in the interiors of the Whitney polygons form a countable collection of pairwise disjoint open subarcs. Applying \eqref{eq:bilip-pieces} to these subarcs and summing gives the required length estimate on the portion of $\gamma$ outside $T$. On the remaining portion, the same estimate follows from $F|_T=g$, which is $L$-bi-Lipschitz. By the additivity of length, $\ell(F\circ\gamma)\leq L'\ell(\gamma).$ The same argument applies to $F^{-1}$, since its restrictions to $T'$ and to the corresponding Whitney polygons are uniformly bi-Lipschitz. Hence,
\[ \ell(\gamma) =\ell(F^{-1}\circ(F\circ\gamma)) \leq L'\ell(F\circ\gamma),\]
and therefore
\[ (L')^{-1}\ell(\gamma) \leq\ell(F\circ\gamma) \leq L'\ell(\gamma).\]
Thus $F$ is an $L'$-BLD homeomorphism of $\mathbb R^2$. Hence, $F$ is bi-Lipschitz, with constant depending only on the bounded turning constant of $T$ and on $L$.
<!-- end proof-27 -->
<!-- thm-7: body_md -->
\label{thm:quantitative version}
Given $C\geq 1$, $\epsilon>0$, and $m\in\{2,3,\dots\}$, there exists $c=c(m)\geq 1$ and $K=K(C,m,\varepsilon)\geq 1$ with the following property. Let be $T\subset \mathbb{R}^2$ a $C$-bounded turning tree with $C$-uniform branch separation, and $\operatorname{Val}(T)\leq m$. Then there exists a $K$-quasiconformal map $F:\mathbb{R}^2\to\mathbb{R}^2$ such that $F(T)$ is a semi-geodesic tree with constant $c$ and with $\dim_H{F(T)}<1+\epsilon$.
<!-- end thm-7 -->
<!-- proof-28: body_md -->
Fix constants $C$, $\epsilon$, $m$, and let $T$ be a tree as in the statement. By Proposition \ref{prop: enumeration of T=enumeration of Tmeta} there exists a $\theta$-quasisymmetric branch-orientation-preserving map $g:T\to \mathcal{T}^{\mathcal{W}}$ with $\dim_H\mathcal{T}^{\mathcal{W}}<1+\epsilon$, where $\theta$ depends only on $C,\epsilon,m$ and $\mathcal{T}^\mathcal{W}$ is semi-geodesic with constant $c$. Theorem \ref{thm: intro 2 quant.} asserts now the existence of a $K$-quasiconformal map $F:\mathbb{R}^2\to \mathbb{R}^2$ such that $F(T)=g(T)=\mathcal{T}^{\mathcal{W}}$ and $K$ depends only on $m$, $C$, and $\epsilon$.
<!-- end proof-28 -->
<!-- prop-5: body_md -->
\label{prop: uniformization of T^4}
A planar tree $T\subset \mathbb{R}^2$ is $K$-quasiconformally equivalent to $\mathcal{T}^4$ if and only if there exist $C\geq 1$ and a sequence $({\bf X}^n)_{n\in\mathbb N_0}$ of finite covers of $T$ with the following properties.
\begin{enumerate}
\item For each $n\in\mathbb{N}_0$, ${\bf X}^n$ is a collection of nondegenerate subtrees of $T$ with ${\bf X}^0 =\{T\}$.
\item If $n\in\mathbb N_0$ and $X\in\mathbf X^n$, then there exists $b_X\in X\setminus\partial X$ such that $X\setminus\{b_X\}$ has exactly four components. Their closures are precisely the four members of $\mathbf X^{n+1}$ contained in $X$. 
\item If $n\in\mathbb{N}$ and $X\in\mathbf X^n$, then $\operatorname{card}(\partial X)\in\{1,2\}$. If $\partial X=\{p_X,q_X\}$, then $p_X$ and $q_X$ belong to opposite branches of $T$ at $b_X$.
\item If $n\in\mathbb{N}$ and $X\in {\bf X}^n$, then there exists $X'\in {\bf X}^{n-1}$ such that $X\subset X'$.
\item If $n\in\mathbb{N}$, $X,Y \in {\bf X}^n$ are distinct, and $X\cap Y \neq \emptyset$, then they intersect on a point of $\partial X\cap \partial Y$ and $\operatorname{diam}{X} \leq C\operatorname{diam}{Y}$.
\item If $n\in\mathbb{N}$, $X,Y \in {\bf X}^n$, and $X\cap Y = \emptyset$, then $\operatorname{dist}(X,Y) \geq C^{-1}\operatorname{diam}{X}$.
\item If $n\in\mathbb{N}_0$, $X\in {\bf X}^n$, $Y\in {\bf X}^{n+1}$, and $X\cap Y \neq \emptyset$, then 
\[ C^{-1}\operatorname{diam}{Y} \leq \operatorname{diam}{X} \leq C\operatorname{diam}{Y}.\]
\item If $n,m \in \mathbb{N}_0$, $X\in {\bf X}^n$, $Y\in {\bf X}^m$, with $m\geq n+C$ and $X\cap Y\neq \emptyset$, then $\operatorname{diam}{Y} \leq \frac12\operatorname{diam}{X}$.
\end{enumerate}
Constants $K$ and $C$ are quantitatively related.
<!-- end prop-5 -->
<!-- proof-29: body_md -->
Assume first that there exists a $K$-quasiconformal map $f:\mathbb{R}^2\to\mathbb{R}^2$ with $f(\mathcal{T}^4)=T$. For each $n\in\mathbb N_0$, set ${\bf X}^n=\{f(\mathcal T_w^4):w\in A_4^n\}$. We show that $({\bf X}^n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}_0}$ satisfies properties (1)--(8). Properties (1)--(4) are immediate by design of the family $({\bf X}^n)_{n\in\mathbb{N}_0}$ and the fact that $f$ is a homeomorphism. 

For (5) suppose that $u,v\in A_4^n$ are distinct and $\mathcal T_u^4\cap\mathcal T_v^4\neq\emptyset$. Then $R_u\cap R_v\neq\emptyset$. By Lemma \ref{lem: properties of R_m}, $u=wi\alpha$, $v=wj\beta$ where $i,j\in\{1,3\}$ and $\alpha,\beta$ are either $\varepsilon$ or of the form $31^{(k)}$ for some $k\geq 0$. By the definition of similarities $\psi_1,\dots,\psi_4$ in \eqref{eq:psi}, we have that 
\[ \operatorname{diam}{\mathcal{T}_u} = \operatorname{diam}(\psi_u(R))\simeq_{\epsilon}\operatorname{diam}(\psi_v(R)) =\operatorname{diam}{\mathcal{T}_v}.\]
By quasiconformality of $f$, $\operatorname{diam}{f(\mathcal{T}_u)} \simeq_{K,\varepsilon} \operatorname{diam}{f(\mathcal{T}_v)}$.

For (6), suppose that $\mathcal T_u^4\cap\mathcal T_v^4=\emptyset$ for some $u,v\in A_4^n$. We argue as in the proof of Lemma \ref{lem:dictqs}. Let $x,y$ such that $\operatorname{dist}(\mathcal{T}^4_u,\mathcal{T}^4_v)=|x-y|$. The arc $\mathcal{T}^4[x,y]$ contains a subarc $I$ crossing a rhombus of the form $R_{u'1}$ or $R_{v'4}$ with 
\[ \operatorname{diam}{I}\gtrsim_{\epsilon}\max\{\operatorname{diam}{\mathcal T_u^4},\operatorname{diam}{\mathcal T_v^4}\}.\]
